4. **FR-9.4 (one scoped escape sentinel per guard):** Each of the six guards MUST additionally support its own environment-variable escape, checked inside the handler (not the wrapper), distinct from `SDLC_DISABLED_HOOKS`, so a single legitimate exception does not require disabling the guard's other checks for the rest of the session: `SDLC_ALLOW_GIT_GUARD=1` (FR-2.10), `SDLC_ALLOW_SHRINK=1` (FR-3.5), `SDLC_ALLOW_UNREAD_EDIT=1` (FR-4.6), `SDLC_ALLOW_CONFIG_EDIT=1` (FR-5.6), `SDLC_ALLOW_SUBAGENT_WRITE=1` (FR-6.5), `SDLC_ALLOW_CHANGELOG_SHAPE=1` (FR-7.7).
5. **FR-9.5 (concrete, self-sufficient remedy — zero external-document dependency):** Every `deny`/`block` reason this feature's guards return MUST (a) state the specific next action to take (e.g., "switch to a feature branch: `git checkout -b feat/<slug>`", "set `SDLC_ALLOW_CONFIG_EDIT=1` if this slice legitimately changes eslint config") — never merely restate which rule was violated — and (b) be fully self-sufficient, readable and actionable with zero reliance on any document outside the reason string itself. Clause (b) exists because guards ship inside the plugin (`hooks/`), while `src/rules/error-recovery.md` ships separately via `install.sh` to `~/.claude/rules/`; a plugin-only adopter who never ran `install.sh` has no such file in their memory layer at all, so a reason reading only "see error-recovery.md" or "this is a Rule 3 violation" without restating what that means is broken for exactly that adopter.
6. **FR-9.6 (deviation-rule tier, embedded as a literal token — mechanical, not aspirational):** Every `deny`/`block` reason MUST embed a literal, machine-parseable tier token in the exact form `[deviation: rule-N — <one-line remedy summary>, <free|costs 1 retry|escalate>]`, chosen by the guard author at write time so the model is never required to re-derive the tier from `src/rules/error-recovery.md` prose — prose which, per FR-9.5(b), it may not even have in context. Mapping: git-guard's branch/`--no-verify`/bulk-add/attribution/conventional-commit checks (FR-2.2–FR-2.6) embed `rule-1`; git-guard's push check (FR-2.7) embeds `rule-3`; shrink-guard (FR-3.3) embeds `rule-1` (its default remedy — write the content in full — is self-correcting; the same reason string also names the `SDLC_ALLOW_SHRINK=1` alternative without a second token); read-guard (FR-4.3) embeds `rule-1`; config-protection (FR-5.2/FR-5.3) embeds `rule-3`; isolation-guard (FR-6.2) embeds `rule-3`; changelog-guard (FR-7.2 failures) embeds `rule-1`. Verbatim example: a shrink-guard denial ends with `[deviation: rule-1 — write the full content, free]`; a config-protection denial ends with `[deviation: rule-3 — set SDLC_ALLOW_CONFIG_EDIT=1, costs 1 retry]`.
7. **FR-9.7 (no self-granted exceptions):** No guard, and no hook shipped by this feature, MUST set its own escape sentinel or its own `SDLC_DISABLED_HOOKS` entry. Escapes and kill switches are set only by the environment the model's own `Bash` tool call can export for subsequent tool calls in the same session — a guard cannot grant itself a permanent exception.
8. **FR-9.8 (exit code 2 is never used anywhere in this harness):** No file under `hooks/` — not `run-hook.js`, not any of Section 7's 3 handlers, not any of this feature's 6 guard handlers — MUST ever call `process.exit(2)` or set `process.exitCode = 2`, for any reason, under any hook event. This is deliberate, not an oversight: exit code 2's meaning is event-dependent in Claude Code's own hook protocol (blocking on `PreToolUse`, merely advisory noise on `PostToolUse`), which is actively dangerous for `pre:edit:read-guard` — the one handler this feature registers under two different events — because the same exit code would mean two different things depending on which registration produced it. Restricting every hook process, blocking or not, to the single invariant "always exits 0, the decision (if any) lives entirely in stdout JSON" (FR-1.10) preserves Section 7's entire fail-open test suite unchanged and makes "deny versus malfunction" auditable by one rule across all 9 hooks this harness now ships, rather than two different rules for two different hook classes.

#### FR-10: CI Regression Assertion Narrowing and Runtime Deny Verification

Section 7's own regression guard (its FR-3.5, implemented as the static source sweep in `tests/hooks/test-wrapper.js`) currently asserts no file anywhere under `hooks/` contains an `exit(2)` path or the string `permissionDecision`. With the wrapper deny channel (FR-1.6–FR-1.10) now living inside `run-hook.js` itself, that file legitimately contains `permissionDecision` — the unnarrowed sweep would fail by design the moment FR-1's slice lands. This FR narrows the string-presence half of the sweep, keeps the exit-code-2 half of it repository-wide (exit(2) remains banned everywhere, per FR-9.8), and replaces source-grep as the guards' own positive control with runtime fixtures — a static grep cannot detect a deny the wrapper silently swallows; only driving a real call through `run-hook.js` and inspecting its actual stdout can.

1. **FR-10.1 (narrowed sweep, split by what changed and what did not):** `tests/hooks/test-wrapper.js`'s static source sweep MUST be split into two independently-scoped checks: (a) the **no-`exit(2)`** check remains repository-wide across every file under `hooks/`, including `run-hook.js` and all 6 of this feature's guard handlers — exit code 2 is banned everywhere per FR-9.8, and this feature makes that invariant strictly more important, not less; (b) the **no-`permissionDecision`/no-`decision: "block"`** check is narrowed to scan only Section 7's 3 original handler files (`session-start-spine.js`, `post-edit-accumulate.js`, `stop-typecheck-format.js`), explicitly excluding both `hooks/lib/run-hook.js` (which now legitimately constructs these fields per FR-1.7) and this feature's 6 guard handlers (whose deny capability is instead proven by the runtime checks in FR-10.2, not by a string's absence). The narrowing MUST be a change to check (b)'s file list, not a deletion of either check — Section 7's non-blocking guarantee for its 3 original handlers remains a real, CI-enforced invariant after this feature ships.
2. **FR-10.2 (runtime deny verification, replacing source-grep as the positive control):** For each of the 6 guards, runtime fixture tests MUST run a real invocation through `run-hook.js` — never grep its source — and assert on actual stdout:
   - **(a) Positive control:** a crafted stdin payload known to trigger that guard's deny condition MUST be driven through `run-hook.js`, and the resulting stdout MUST contain the correct decision field for that guard's event — `hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision: "deny"` for the 5 `PreToolUse`-blocking guards (`pre:bash:git-guard`, `pre:write:shrink-guard`, `pre:edit:read-guard`'s `PreToolUse` half, `pre:edit:config-protection`, `pre:agent:isolation-guard`), or `decision: "block"` for `stop:changelog-guard`.
   - **(b) Negative control (fail-open still holds for a deny-capable guard):** an injected-malfunction fixture (a simulated handler throw, matching Section 7 FR-8.3's existing fail-open fixture pattern) driven through `run-hook.js` for the same guard MUST result in stdout containing no `permissionDecision` field and no `decision: "block"` field, with exit code `0` — proving a malfunctioning deny-capable guard still fails open exactly like Section 7's 3 hooks always have.
   - **(c) Structural incapability of the `PostToolUse` half (`pre:edit:read-guard` specifically):** a dedicated fixture MUST feed a `deny`-shaped return value (`{ deny: { reason: ... } }`) to the shared handler under a simulated `PostToolUse`/`Read` invocation and assert the resulting stdout contains no decision field of any kind — proving FR-1.7's event-aware drop holds even if a future dispatch bug in the shared handler module caused it to construct a `deny` result under the wrong event.
3. **FR-10.3:** FR-10.1's two sweeps and FR-10.2's runtime checks (a), (b), and (c) MUST all run in the same CI job (`validate-assets`) that already runs Section 7's hook fixture tests, so a regression in any direction — a Section 7 handler gaining a deny path, `run-hook.js` gaining an `exit(2)` path, a guard losing its deny capability, a guard losing its fail-open behavior, or the `PostToolUse` drop rule breaking — fails CI.
4. **FR-10.4 (implementation ordering constraint, binding on the planner's slice sequencing):** FR-1.6 through FR-1.10 (the wrapper deny channel) and FR-10.1's narrowed sweep MUST land, and MUST pass CI, in a slice (or slices) committed **before** any guard handler from FR-2 through FR-7 is committed. Committing a guard handler first, against the still-unnarrowed Section 7 sweep, would fail CI the moment `run-hook.js` gains its deny-serialization logic (a `permissionDecision` string appears where the old sweep still forbids it), or would ship a guard with no wrapper channel yet built to carry its deny. Either ordering violates the one-slice-one-green-commit rule this pipeline otherwise guarantees. The planner MUST sequence the wrapper-channel-and-sweep-narrowing work into an early wave with no guard-handler slice as a sibling or as a predecessor-violating dependency.

#### FR-11: Autonomy Regression Test

The single most important requirement in this section — every guard above is worthless if, in aggregate, they stall an unattended run.

1. **FR-11.1:** With all 6 guards enabled at the `standard` profile (FR-1.5) and no `SDLC_DISABLED_HOOKS` entries or per-guard escape sentinels (FR-9.4) set, a full `/develop-feature` run on a seeded feature in this repository MUST reach `MERGE READY` with **zero human interventions** — no guard denial the model cannot self-resolve through the deviation rules (FR-9.6) may occur during the run.
2. **FR-11.2:** The regression run MUST be a repeatable, scripted test (not a one-off manual observation), and its pass/fail result MUST be recorded in this feature's implementation record, satisfying the roadmap's cross-cutting verification requirement ("Autonomy regression test after F2b").

### 8.4 Non-Functional Requirements

1. **NFR-1 (zero dependencies, inherited unchanged):** All 6 guard handler modules MUST have zero npm runtime dependencies, consistent with Section 7 NFR-1 — no `npm install` step is required for any guard to run. Subprocess use (e.g., `pre:bash:git-guard`'s `git rev-parse`, `stop:changelog-guard`'s `git status --porcelain`) MUST use Node's built-in `child_process` module only. The wrapper's new deny-serialization logic (FR-1.6–FR-1.10) likewise introduces no dependency — it is built-in `JSON.stringify`/object-literal logic identical in kind to Section 7's existing `finish()`.
2. **NFR-2 (ES5 syntax floor — scope extended to the wrapper's new logic, not merely restated):** Section 7 FR-2.6 established that `hooks/lib/run-hook.js` **in its entirety** — not merely the code that executes before the version gate — MUST parse under the oldest plausible Node an adopter's machine might resolve; Node parses a required file as one unit before any statement in it executes, so a `SyntaxError` anywhere in the file prevents the gate itself from ever running, regardless of how far below the gate the offending syntax textually sits. This feature adds the FR-1.6–FR-1.10 deny-serialization logic directly inside `run-hook.js`'s existing `finish()`/`emit()` functions, plus 6 entries to the `HOOKS` id-to-handler map (already ES5-safe object literals) — all of it MUST be written in the same ES5 syntax floor as the rest of the file (`var`, function declarations, string concatenation; no arrow functions, template literals, `const`/`let`, destructuring, optional chaining, or `async`/`await`). This constraint binds `run-hook.js`'s own source only. The 6 guard handler modules themselves are separate files, `require`'d only after the gate passes, and MAY use modern syntax freely — exactly as Section 7's 3 handlers already do.
3. **NFR-3 (backward compatible, no autonomy regression):** A project without the plugin installed, run with `SDLC_HOOKS_ENABLED=0`, or run with all 6 guard ids listed in `SDLC_DISABLED_HOOKS`, behaves identically to a Section-7-only installation with no guards configured — no observable behavior change beyond the guards themselves. Every guard is additive and independently disableable; none of Section 7's 3 hooks changes behavior as a result of this feature.
4. **NFR-4 (asset budget):** This feature adds 6 hooks (the ids in FR-2 through FR-7) to Section 7's 3, for a running total of **9 hooks** against the v4.0 hard budget of **≤12 hooks** (Section 6 NFR-5). Agent count remains 13, skill count remains 5 — unchanged from Section 6/7. This feature consumes 6 of the remaining 9 hook slots under the cap; **3 remain** for the roadmap's F3/F4/F5, one of which the deferred `pre:edit:gateguard` (Design Decision 9) would consume if and when a future feature picks it back up.

### 8.5 Acceptance Criteria

1. **AC-1:** On this repository's own `main` branch, a `Bash` tool call `git commit -m "feat(core): x"` is denied by `pre:bash:git-guard` with a reason naming the branch problem and a concrete remedy (create/switch to a feature branch); the identical call issued on a feature branch is not denied.
2. **AC-2:** `git commit --no-verify -m "feat(core): x"` on a feature branch is denied; the same command without `--no-verify` is not denied.
3. **AC-3:** `git add -A` and `git add .` are each denied; `git add path/to/file.js` is not denied.
4. **AC-4:** `git commit -m "feat(core): x\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>"` is denied; the identical message without the attribution line is not denied.
5. **AC-5:** `git commit -m "docs(readme): x"` (scope not in `api|ui|db|auth|core|infra`) is denied; `git commit -m "refactor(core): x"` (type not in `feat|fix|test|chore`) is denied; `git commit -m "feat(core): x"` is not denied.
6. **AC-6:** A plain `git push` is denied unless `SDLC_ALLOW_GIT_GUARD=1` is set, in which case it is not denied. A `git push --force` remains denied by `permissions.deny` regardless of this guard or its escape (FR-1.4/FR-2.8).
7. **AC-7:** A `Write` to `docs/PRD.md` that reduces its line count below `max(oldLines × 0.4, 40)` is denied by `pre:write:shrink-guard`; the identical `Write` is not denied when `SDLC_ALLOW_SHRINK=1` is set. A `Write` that grows the file, or shrinks it by less than the threshold, is not denied.
8. **AC-8:** An `Edit` to an existing file that has not been `Read` earlier in the same session is denied by `pre:edit:read-guard`; the identical `Edit` is allowed after a prior `Read` tool call against the same file in the same session. A `Write` creating a brand-new file (no prior on-disk content) is never denied by this guard, regardless of read history.
9. **AC-9:** An `Edit` to `tsconfig.json` flipping `"strict": true` to `"strict": false` is denied by `pre:edit:config-protection`; the identical `Edit` is allowed when `SDLC_ALLOW_CONFIG_EDIT=1` is set. An `Edit` inserting `@ts-nocheck` at the top of a `.ts` source file that did not previously contain it is denied under the same escape.
10. **AC-10:** An `Edit` to a file under `tests/fixtures/**`, `docs/**`, or matching `*.md` that introduces the literal string `@ts-nocheck` or an unscoped `eslint-disable` is never denied by `pre:edit:config-protection`, regardless of content — verifying FR-5.3's scope exclusion, and specifically exercised by the autonomy regression run (AC-18), where this feature's own QA docs and fixtures legitimately contain these strings.
11. **AC-11:** If the FR-6.1 spike confirms a reliable subagent indicator: a `Write` to `.claude/scratchpad.md` or `CHANGELOG.md` made from a parallel-wave subagent context is denied by `pre:agent:isolation-guard`; the identical `Write` from the orchestrator's own context is not denied. If the spike finds no reliable indicator: every such `Write`, from any context, produces the FR-6.3 visible warning, unconditionally, every time — verifiable by a fixture asserting the warning fires on both a simulated subagent call and a simulated orchestrator call identically.
12. **AC-12:** A response in which `CHANGELOG.md` was modified but the new entry is missing a `**Details:**` line, exceeds 500 characters in Details, or duplicates an existing entry name under today's date causes `stop:changelog-guard` to return a blocking `Stop` decision — `decision: "block"` with a `reason`, delivered through the wrapper's deny channel per FR-1.7, never an exit code — identifying the specific defect; an entry meeting all shape requirements does not block `Stop`, regardless of elapsed wall-clock time since the entry's stated `HH:MM UTC`. A fixture asserting 3 consecutive failing checks in one session confirms the guard stops blocking after 2 (FR-7.5) and instead emits a non-blocking warning on the third.
13. **AC-13:** Every deny/block reason captured across AC-1 through AC-12 contains a literal `[deviation: rule-N — ...]` token matching FR-9.6's mapping, and none contains an unresolved reference to an external document (e.g., no bare mention of `error-recovery.md` without inline restatement of what it means) — verified by a fixture assertion scanning each captured reason string, per FR-9.5/FR-9.6.
14. **AC-14:** For each of the 6 guards, the FR-10.2(a) positive-control runtime fixture shows the correct decision field in stdout (`hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision: "deny"` or `decision: "block"`, as applicable) for a known deny case driven through `run-hook.js`; the FR-10.2(b) negative-control runtime fixture (a simulated handler throw) shows no decision field of either kind and exit code `0` for the same guard.
15. **AC-15:** The FR-10.2(c) fixture — a `deny`-shaped handler return value fed to `pre:edit:read-guard`'s shared handler under a simulated `PostToolUse`/`Read` invocation — produces stdout with no `permissionDecision` field and no `decision: "block"` field, and exit code `0`.
16. **AC-16:** FR-10.1(a)'s repository-wide no-`exit(2)` check continues to exit `0` scanning every file under `hooks/`, including `run-hook.js` and all 6 new guard handlers. FR-10.1(b)'s narrowed no-`permissionDecision`/no-`decision: "block"` check exits `0` scanning only Section 7's 3 original handler files, and its file list — inspected directly in `tests/hooks/test-wrapper.js` — explicitly excludes both `run-hook.js` and all 6 new guard handlers.
17. **AC-17:** `SDLC_DISABLED_HOOKS` set to a single comma-separated value containing all 6 of this feature's ids results in zero denials and zero guard-originated `systemMessage`s across a full pass through every guarded tool-call shape exercised in AC-1 through AC-12.
18. **AC-18 (the section's primary acceptance criterion):** A full `/develop-feature` run on a seeded feature in this repository, with all 6 guards enabled at the `standard` profile and no escape sentinels set, reaches `MERGE READY` with zero human interventions — recorded as a scripted, repeatable test result (FR-11.2), not a one-off observation.
19. **AC-19:** Post-feature hook handler count is exactly 9 (`ls hooks/handlers/*.js | wc -l` including Section 7's 3 plus this feature's 6 — `pre:edit:read-guard`'s dual registration is one handler file, not two), verifiable against the v4.0 budget of ≤12 (NFR-4).
20. **AC-20:** `git log` for this feature shows the wrapper-deny-channel-and-sweep-narrowing commit(s) (FR-1.6–FR-1.10, FR-10.1) preceding every guard-handler commit (FR-2 through FR-7) — verifiable by commit order, and by re-running CI at the wrapper-channel commit alone, with zero guard handlers yet present, and confirming it passes green (FR-10.4).

### 8.6 Affected Components

#### New Files

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `hooks/handlers/pre-bash-git-guard.js` | `pre:bash:git-guard` — the five git-hygiene checks plus the push-intent check (FR-2) |
| `hooks/handlers/pre-write-shrink-guard.js` | `pre:write:shrink-guard` — curated-file shrink-ratio check (FR-3) |
| `hooks/handlers/pre-edit-read-guard.js` | `pre:edit:read-guard` — both halves (`PostToolUse` `Read` recorder, `PreToolUse` `Edit\|Write` gate), dispatched on `input.hook_event_name`; the recorder half is structurally incapable of emitting a decision regardless of what it returns (FR-1.7, FR-4) |
| `hooks/lib/read-tracker.js` | Session-keyed "read this session" record, reusing `hooks/lib/accumulator.js`'s directory/sanitization/GC pattern (FR-4.2) |
| `hooks/handlers/pre-edit-config-protection.js` | `pre:edit:config-protection` — config-weakening and `@ts-nocheck`/blanket-`eslint-disable` checks, scoped away from `docs/**`, `*.md`, and `tests/fixtures/**` (FR-5) |
| `hooks/handlers/pre-agent-isolation-guard.js` | `pre:agent:isolation-guard` — subagent-origin scratchpad/changelog write block, or its fail-visible fallback (FR-6) |
| `hooks/handlers/stop-changelog-guard.js` | `stop:changelog-guard` — changelog entry shape + idempotency assertion, bounded blocking (FR-7) |
| `tests/hooks/test-guards.js` | Runtime fixture tests implementing FR-10.2(a)/(b)/(c): per-guard positive deny control, per-guard fail-open negative control, and `pre:edit:read-guard`'s `PostToolUse` structural-incapability check |
| `tests/fixtures/hooks/guards/*` | Crafted stdin fixtures for every guard's positive (denied), negative (allowed), escape-sentinel, and fail-open cases, feeding `tests/hooks/test-guards.js` (FR-9, FR-10.2, FR-11) |

*(Exact handler and lib filenames are illustrative and may be finalized by the planner during `/bootstrap-feature`, per Section 7's own precedent; `hooks/hooks.json` id strings in FR-2 through FR-7 are fixed.)*

#### Modified Files

| File | Changes | Related Requirements |
|------|---------|---------------------|
| `hooks/hooks.json` | Add `PreToolUse` entries matched `Bash` (git-guard), `Write` (shrink-guard), and `Edit\|Write` (read-guard's gate half, config-protection, isolation-guard); add a `PostToolUse` entry matched `Read` (read-guard's recorder half, same id as its `PreToolUse` entry); add a second entry to the existing `Stop` array (changelog-guard, alongside Section 7's `stop:typecheck-format`) | FR-2 through FR-7 |
| `hooks/lib/run-hook.js` | Gains the deny-serialization channel inside its existing `finish()`/`emit()` logic — a structural contract change to the shared dispatcher, not merely additive data (FR-1.6 through FR-1.10) — plus 6 entries in the `HOOKS` id-to-handler map (FR-1.5). All new logic stays within the file's existing ES5 syntax floor (NFR-2): the whole file is parsed as one unit before the version gate can run, so "modern syntax after the gate" is not a safe pattern within this file itself. | FR-1.5 through FR-1.10, FR-2 through FR-7, NFR-2 |
| `tests/hooks/test-wrapper.js` | Splits its static sweep in two: the no-`exit(2)` check stays repository-wide across all of `hooks/`; the no-`permissionDecision`/no-`decision: "block"` check narrows to Section 7's 3 original handler files only, explicitly excluding `run-hook.js` | FR-10.1 |
| `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | Extend `validate-assets` to run `tests/hooks/test-guards.js`'s runtime fixtures (FR-10.2) alongside the split `test-wrapper.js` sweep (FR-10.1) and Section 7's existing invocations | FR-10.3 |

#### Unchanged Files (verified no impact)

| File | Reason |
|------|--------|
| `templates/settings.json` | This feature's guards operate entirely inside Claude Code's hook layer; the fail-closed `permissions.deny`/`allow` lists Section 7 FR-7 shipped are unaffected and remain the boundary for irreversible actions (FR-1.4) |
| `.gitignore` (repo root), `templates/.gitignore` | `.claude/tmp/` is already covered by Section 7 FR-6.5; the new read-tracker record file lands in the same already-ignored directory, requiring no additional entry |
| `agents/*.md` (13 files) | No agent role or prompt changes — the guards are hook-layer enforcement, not agent-prompt changes |
| `skills/*/SKILL.md` (5 files) | No skill changes — every guard fires automatically from existing `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse`/`Stop` events; no new command surface is introduced |
| `src/rules/git.md`, `src/rules/changelog.md`, `src/rules/scratchpad.md` | The prose rules these guards mechanize are unchanged in wording; this feature adds mechanical enforcement alongside them, it does not rewrite or replace them |

### 8.7 UI Changes

None. This feature adds `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse`/`Stop` hook handlers that run inside Claude Code's own tool-call and session lifecycle, identical in kind to Section 7's hooks; there is no web application, page, or user-facing component. The only observable surface is a `deny`/`block` reason or `systemMessage` text delivered into the agent's own turn, not a UI.

### 8.8 Schema Changes

None. This project has no database of any kind; nothing in this feature introduces one. The session-keyed state file this feature adds (`hooks/lib/read-tracker.js`) is a plain-text record under `.claude/tmp/`, not a schema.

### 8.9 Affected Endpoints

None. This project has no API server; guards execute as local child processes spawned directly by Claude Code's own hook engine on the developer's machine, exactly as Section 7's hooks do.

### 8.10 Risks and Dependencies

1. **Risk: A guard wedging an unattended run (the top risk).** Any of the 6 guards denying a call the model cannot self-resolve would stall the pipeline exactly where the autonomy contract forbids. Mitigation: every guard has a scoped escape sentinel plus a `SDLC_DISABLED_HOOKS` entry (FR-9.3, FR-9.4), every deny reason states a concrete, self-sufficient remedy carrying a literal deviation-rule token (FR-9.5, FR-9.6), `stop:changelog-guard`'s Stop-blocking is explicitly bounded rather than able to loop (FR-7.5), the wrapper's own deny channel is exercised by dedicated runtime fixtures rather than assumed correct (FR-10.2), and the autonomy regression test (FR-11, AC-18) is the section's primary, hard acceptance gate — a feature that fails it is not done regardless of how correct each guard's individual logic is.
2. **Risk: False positives on legitimate work, especially `config-protection`.** A slice that deliberately, correctly relaxes a lint rule as part of its actual scope would otherwise be blocked indistinguishably from an unattended run cheating a red build green. Mitigation: FR-5.6's escape (`SDLC_ALLOW_CONFIG_EDIT=1`) plus FR-9.5's concrete-remedy requirement means the guard's own message tells the model exactly how to proceed; FR-5.3's scope exclusion (`docs/**`, `*.md`, `tests/fixtures/**`) additionally prevents the guard from firing on this feature's own documentation and fixtures; Gate 2 (Code Review, FR-5.8's backstop) would still catch a wrongly-bypassed weakening at merge.
3. **Risk: Subagent-indicator uncertainty (`pre:agent:isolation-guard`).** Whether `PreToolUse` stdin reliably distinguishes subagent-originated calls is unverified going into this feature. Mitigation: FR-6.1's mandatory spike is a go/no-go gate before any blocking code is written; FR-6.3's fail-visible fallback ensures the enforcement gap is surfaced loudly rather than silently unenforced if the spike comes back negative — and FR-6.6 makes clear that the visible-warning branch, if that is where the spike lands, still satisfies this section's acceptance criteria on its own terms.
4. **Deferred: `pre:edit:gateguard` is out of scope for this feature.** It was originally scoped as this section's seventh guard; it is deferred to its own later feature. Rationale, recorded here per the architecture review: it mechanizes a hypothesis rather than an existing prose rule (all six shipped guards mechanize a rule already known to matter); being opt-in (`SDLC_GATEGUARD=on`), shipping it inside this feature would not actually start its own 10-slice promotion-measurement window, since nothing forces any project to turn it on; it would have dragged in a second session-scoped state library and a dependency on the same wrapper refactor FR-1 already requires; and it would have added variance to the autonomy regression test (FR-11) this feature lives or dies by. When a future feature picks it back up, it consumes one of the 3 hook slots this feature leaves under the v4.0 cap (NFR-4), and inherits FR-1's wrapper deny channel and FR-9's cross-guard requirements (including the FR-9.6 literal deviation token) unchanged.
5. **Risk: `git-guard`'s regex/parsing logic could silently diverge from `src/rules/git.md`'s prose if the rule file is edited independently in the future.** A future edit to the allowed commit types/scopes or the attribution-phrase list in `src/rules/git.md` would not automatically update the guard's own matching logic. Mitigation: FR-2's intro explicitly cross-references the five source rules by content; this is recorded here as a standing maintenance dependency between the prose rule and its mechanized counterpart, not solved by this feature alone.
6. **Risk: `stop:changelog-guard`'s Stop-event blocking semantics are less familiar than `PreToolUse` deny and could be mis-implemented as if it could prevent the write that already happened.** Mitigation: FR-7.4 states precisely what "blocking" means for this hook (forcing another turn via the wrapper's `decision: "block"` channel, never a process exit code — FR-9.2, FR-9.8), and FR-7.5's 2-block cap bounds worst-case behavior even if the shape check itself has a bug that never resolves.
7. **Risk: `pre:edit:read-guard`'s dual-event registration (one id, two `hooks.json` entries) is an architecture pattern Section 7 never needed.** A miswired registration (e.g., the `PostToolUse` recorder half accidentally matched on the wrong tool, or the two halves dispatching to different handler files) would make the guard either silently useless (never records, so every edit passes) or, worse, capable of leaking a decision from the wrong event. Mitigation: FR-1.7's event-aware wrapper-side drop rule is a *structural* guarantee, not handler discipline — only `PreToolUse`/`Stop` events can ever produce a decision, regardless of what the shared handler returns. FR-10.2(c)'s dedicated runtime fixture and AC-15 verify this directly, and AC-8 additionally requires a fixture proving the record-then-gate sequence works correctly end to end, not merely that leakage is prevented.
8. **Risk: Asset budget headroom.** This feature consumes 6 of the remaining 9 hook slots under the v4.0 cap of 12 (Section 6 NFR-5), leaving 3 for the roadmap's F3/F4/F5 — one of which the deferred `pre:edit:gateguard` (Risk 4) would consume if a future feature picks it back up. Mitigation: none of F3/F4/F5 currently plans a new top-level hook beyond folding new state into `session:start:spine`'s existing injection (the roadmap's F5 description); flagged here as a standing constraint on future roadmap features, not a defect in this one.
9. **Risk: Slice-ordering violation.** If the planner sequences a guard-handler slice (FR-2 through FR-7) before the wrapper-deny-channel-and-sweep-narrowing slice (FR-1.6–FR-1.10, FR-10.1), that guard's own commit would either fail CI outright (the still-unnarrowed Section 7 sweep forbids `permissionDecision` anywhere the moment `run-hook.js` gains it) or ship a guard with no wrapper channel yet built to carry its deny — either way breaking the one-slice-one-green-commit rule this pipeline otherwise guarantees. Mitigation: FR-10.4 states the ordering constraint as a hard requirement, not a suggestion, and AC-20 verifies it directly by re-running CI at the wrapper-channel commit alone, before any guard handler exists.
10. **Dependency: Section 7 (Hook Infrastructure and Non-Blocking Hooks) must exist first.** Every guard in FR-2 through FR-7 is dispatched through `hooks/lib/run-hook.js` and subject to its fail-open contract (FR-1.2); none of this feature's requirements is independently invokable without Section 7's wrapper, configuration file, and runtime controls already in place. This feature additionally extends that same wrapper file with the FR-1.6–FR-1.10 deny channel — the one piece of new shared infrastructure this feature adds beneath Section 7's own code. Per the roadmap's stated execution order (F1 → F2a → F2b), this feature is F2b and lands immediately after Section 7.
11. **Dependency: Section 1 FR-2 (deviation rules).** Every guard's deny/block reason embeds a literal tier token mapped from the existing four deviation rules (FR-9.6); a future material change to `src/rules/error-recovery.md`'s rule definitions or numbering (rule-1 through rule-4) should prompt a review of FR-9.6's mapping, since the tokens are now a hard-coded mechanical dependency on that numbering, not merely a phrasing convention.
12. **Dependency: Section 5 (Changelog Automation).** `stop:changelog-guard`'s shape assertions (FR-7.2) and its backstop (FR-7.6) both depend on `src/rules/changelog.md`'s format being unchanged; `pre:agent:isolation-guard`'s first backstop (FR-6.7) is the same idempotency guard Section 5 FR-1.6 defines.
13. **Dependency: Mandatory `security-auditor` pre-review — `pre:agent:isolation-guard` (FR-6).** Whichever branch the FR-6.1 spike lands on, this guard's correctness (or its fail-visible fallback) is security-relevant: an unenforced subagent-isolation rule is exactly the kind of gap a hostile or careless parallel-wave prompt could exploit to corrupt shared state. This slice REQUIRES `security-auditor` review before merge, scheduled as a pre-review during `/bootstrap-feature`, mirroring the posture Section 7 established for `session:start:spine` and `stop:typecheck-format`.
14. **Dependency: Mandatory `security-auditor` pre-review — `pre:bash:git-guard` (FR-2).** This guard parses and acts on live `Bash` command strings and spawns its own `git` subprocess to resolve branch state — a similarly shaped surface to Section 7's `stop:typecheck-format` (FR-6.14 there required the same pre-review for executing a project-declared command). This slice REQUIRES `security-auditor` review before merge for the same reason: command-string parsing that gets the boundary wrong is a security-relevant defect, not merely a functional one.
15. **Dependency: Mandatory `security-auditor` pre-review — the wrapper deny channel (FR-1.6–FR-1.10).** `run-hook.js` is the shared dispatcher every guard, and every Section 7 hook, is invoked through; a defect in its event-aware serialization (FR-1.7) — a missed event-name case that lets a deny leak out where it should not, or a malformed-deny path that is not actually dropped (FR-1.9) — would be a single point of failure across all 9 hooks this harness ships, not one guard's isolated bug. This slice REQUIRES `security-auditor` review before merge, scheduled as a pre-review during `/bootstrap-feature`, and per FR-10.4 must land before any guard-handler slice regardless.
16. **Dependency: Mandatory `security-auditor` pre-review — `stop:changelog-guard` (FR-7).** This guard spawns its own `git status --porcelain` subprocess using a `cwd` resolved from stdin, the same class of surface as `pre:bash:git-guard`'s `git rev-parse` subprocess (Dependency 14) — command and path construction from stdin-derived input is security-relevant regardless of which guard performs it. This slice REQUIRES `security-auditor` review before merge for the same reason.
