## 9. Verification & Review Upgrade

**Status:** [DRAFT]
**Date:** 2026-08-15
**Priority:** High
**Related:** Section 1 (FR-1 — `verifier`'s four inspection levels are unchanged by this feature; only the *Overall* verdict line and Level 2's marker-severity handling change, per FR-1 and FR-4 below. FR-1.2's flat marker list is superseded by FR-4's BLOCKER/WARNING split. FR-1.6's "Gate 5.5, or renumbered appropriately" is confirmed shipped as **Gate 6, "Goal-Backward Verification,"** in `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md` — one of this feature's three autonomy triggers, alongside **Gate 2 "Code Review"** and **Gate 3 "Security Audit,"** all three verified directly against that file rather than assumed from the roadmap's prose. FR-2, the four deviation rules — Gate 6's `--gaps` replan loop (FR-10) and the write-surface refusal (FR-9) are mechanizations of Rule 3/Rule 4, and the Plan Critic's 3-loop-then-escalate mechanism (FR-5.5) resolves into the existing Rule 4 escalation path, not a new one); Section 2 (FR-1.4, the planner's wave summary table — this feature adds a `Files (union)` column, FR-9.1; FR-5, the Plan Critic's Wave Assignment Validation checks — carried forward into `agents/plan-critic.md` unchanged in substance, FR-5.2; FR-2.4, wave N+1 waits for wave N — the mechanism FR-8.5's tracer gate relies on structurally); Section 4 (NFR-4, "no new agents are added... total agent count remains at 13" — this feature adds `agents/plan-critic.md`, bringing the count to 14, a direct, acknowledged conflict — see NFR-6 below. Section 4 itself is not edited by this feature; its revision is deferred to F5 per the roadmap's execution order); Section 6 (NFR-5, "the v4.0 hard budget is ≤16 agents, ≤10 skills, ≤12 hooks" — this feature's 14 agents leave 2 of that headroom, matching the roadmap's own arithmetic, "13 + plan-critic + debugger = 15," one slot spare after F5's `debugger` lands); Section 7 (NFR-1's three-Node-context rule — `scripts/ci/` fail-closed zero-dependency Node, `install.sh` barred from Node entirely, hooks fail-open — this feature's NFR-5 is rescoped to state explicitly that FR-11's CI-zone validator is governed by that existing rule, not a new exception to markdown-only); Section 8 (the `merge-ready` Gate 0–8 table this feature extends without renumbering).

### 9.1 Description

Sections 1 and 2 gave `verifier` four inspection levels and gave the planner wave-parallel slices, but two structural gaps remain, both verified directly against the shipped agent and skill files rather than assumed:

1. `verifier`'s Output Format (`agents/verifier.md`) collapses four levels of finding into a three-state `### Overall: PASS / FAIL / WARN` line. A feature whose code is present, stub-free, and correctly wired (Levels 1–3 all PASS) but whose Level 4 data-flow trace finds no test or trace exercising it currently reports `WARN`, and `WARN` is explicitly documented in the agent's own Constraints as advisory — "Level 4 failures MUST NOT block merge." An unattended run can therefore report `MERGE READY` on behavior nobody has verified actually executes, with no downstream signal distinguishing that outcome from a feature genuinely exercised end-to-end.
2. `verifier`'s output is unstructured prose with no file it writes to — its `tools:` frontmatter is `["Read", "Glob", "Grep"]`, and its Constraints section states, unqualified, "Read-only: you MUST NOT modify any files." Nothing downstream can consume a failed verification programmatically; `/merge-ready`'s Auto-Fix Protocol has to re-derive what to fix from prose, and there is no path from "verification found a gap" back into planning without a human reading the report.
3. The Plan Critic — the pipeline's only adversarial review of a plan before implementation begins — is a 65-line prompt inlined as a markdown blockquote inside `src/claude.md`'s "Plan Critic Pass" section (lines 94–199; the blockquote itself spans lines 102–166). It is unversioned (no frontmatter, no `name`, no `tools`, no `model`), untestable by `scripts/ci/validate-agents.js` (which only scans `agents/*.md`), and cannot be given a tool restriction. Verified directly against `skills/bootstrap-feature/SKILL.md` and `skills/develop-feature/SKILL.md`: neither skill file invokes the Plan Critic at all — its only trigger today is the interactive plan-mode workflow described in `src/claude.md`, run before `ExitPlanMode`. A plan produced entirely autonomously by `/bootstrap-feature` Step 5 during a `/develop-feature` run, with no plan-mode involved, is never critiqued by anything at all today.
4. `code-reviewer` and `security-auditor` report every finding they notice, at any confidence, with no consolidation and no diff-scoping — an unattended run gets blocked by speculative or repetitive findings as often as by real ones.
5. `planner` produces slices with no vertical tracer — an integration mistake in how layers connect surfaces only after every expansion slice is already built — and no declared write surface per wave, so `/develop-feature`'s existing wave-parallel dispatch (Section 2 FR-2.3) has no mechanical check that two slices in the same wave do not, in fact, share a file. Section 2 FR-5.1 gives the Plan Critic a CRITICAL-severity check for this at *plan* time; nothing checks it again at *dispatch* time, after the plan may have been hand-edited or replanned.

**Design Decisions:**
1. **Level 4 stops being purely advisory, without becoming a hard block.** Section 1's Constraints line — "Level 4 failures MUST NOT block merge — they are advisory" — is superseded in effect, not deleted: a Level 4 gap still never produces a `FAILED` verdict on its own, but it now downgrades the overall verdict from `VERIFIED` to `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED`, which (per FR-10.4) does prevent `MERGE READY` until the gap is closed or explicitly accepted. This is not a dead end: FR-10's `--gaps` replan loop is the recovery path, consistent with the autonomy contract.
2. **`verifier` gains exactly one write target, not general write access.** FR-2 adds `Write` to `agents/verifier.md`'s `tools:` frontmatter and narrows the Constraints section's "Read-only" statement to a single named exception (`docs/verification/<feature>.md` only) rather than removing the read-only posture entirely — `verifier` still MUST NOT modify any source file, test file, or other document.
3. **The Plan Critic extraction is wired into both of its real trigger points, not just moved.** Because the audit above found the Plan Critic currently never fires during `/bootstrap-feature`, this feature does two things, not one: (a) extracts the prompt into `agents/plan-critic.md` (FR-5.1–FR-5.4), and (b) adds its invocation to `/bootstrap-feature` Step 5, after the planner produces the plan and before Step 6 (Git Setup) — closing the gap so autonomously-produced plans are critiqued too (FR-5.9). The existing plan-mode trigger in `src/claude.md` is kept and rewritten as a delegation stub (FR-5.6).
4. **BLOCKER/WARNING/INFO supersedes CRITICAL/MAJOR/MINOR terminology, not the underlying checks.** Section 1 FR-4.2 ("MAJOR finding") and Section 2 FR-5.1/FR-5.2 ("CRITICAL finding") used the old scale inside the same inlined prompt this feature moves. FR-5.4 states the literal mapping (CRITICAL→BLOCKER, MAJOR→WARNING, MINOR→INFO) so every check those two sections already specified survives the move under its new name, with no check dropped or weakened.
5. **The `--gaps` replan loop reuses the existing Auto-Fix Protocol; it does not add a second one.** `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md`'s Auto-Fix Protocol already reruns a single failed gate up to 3 times. FR-10 specializes what "fix" means for Gate 6 specifically — feed the `gaps:` array to the `planner` agent rather than have the orchestrating model free-form re-read prose — without adding a parallel retry mechanism.
6. **The write-surface check is a dispatch-time re-verification, not a replacement for the Plan Critic's plan-time check.** Section 2 FR-5.1 already gives the Plan Critic a CRITICAL check for wave file-overlap at plan-authoring time. FR-9 adds the same check again, mechanically, inside `/develop-feature` Phase 2 immediately before the `Agent` tool calls for a wave are issued — catching drift if the plan was hand-edited, replanned via FR-10, or produced by a planner version that skipped critique.
7. **Anti-pattern markers keep their existing scan mechanism; only their severity changes.** `agents/verifier.md`'s Level 2 already greps for `TODO, FIXME, XXX, HACK, placeholder, stub, not implemented, throw new Error('Not implemented'), pass  # TODO, raise NotImplementedError`. FR-4 does not replace this scan; it adds one new token (`TBD`, not previously scanned) and reclassifies every existing token into BLOCKER or WARNING per the table in FR-4.1, with one documented exception (FR-4.4) for the compound `pass  # TODO` pattern, which denotes an empty function body, not deferred cleanup.
8. **`verifier` remains Bash-free; Gate 6 supplies the timestamp.** Confirmed against `agents/verifier.md`'s frontmatter (`Read, Glob, Grep`, now `+Write`, FR-2.1): it has no `Bash` tool and gains none, so it cannot run `date -u` itself. Rather than let it invent a timestamp — which would violate `src/rules/changelog.md`'s never-invent-timestamps rule, extended here to verification reports — Gate 6 (which already has `Bash` per `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md`'s `allowed-tools`) obtains the UTC timestamp and states it, together with the feature slug, verbatim in the delegation prompt to `verifier` (FR-2.7).
9. **`planner` stays read-only; the orchestrator performs every append.** Confirmed against `agents/planner.md`'s frontmatter (`Read, Glob, Grep, WebSearch, WebFetch`): no `Write`/`Edit`. FR-10's replan loop therefore has `planner` return slices, never write them — the orchestrator appends them to the plan file, append-only, mirroring the established `skills/bootstrap-feature/SKILL.md` Step 7 pattern where the orchestrator, not `planner`, performs the write for what `planner` returns.
10. **A missing tracer marker is a caught defect, not a silent fallback.** The original NFR-3 wording ("no marker → no gate") could not distinguish a genuinely legacy plan from a planner that simply forgot the marker on a fresh plan. FR-5.10 closes the gap at the only point every fresh plan already passes through (`plan-critic`, FR-5.6/FR-5.9), and FR-8.6 makes the fallback itself visible rather than silent when it does apply — consistent with Section 8's own standing prohibition on a guard "that looks installed but never runs."
11. **The `Write` grant is deny-by-default wording, not a permission grant, and is deliberately not mechanized as a hook in this feature.** FR-2.2's Constraints text is phrased as a prohibition ("MUST NOT Write to any path other than...") rather than a scope grant, matching how the harness's blocking guards (Section 8) are worded. A generic agent-write-surface hook keyed on `agent_type` — mechanizing this the way Section 8's guards mechanize prose rules — is recorded as the correct future direction (9.10) but is explicitly out of scope for this feature, consistent with Section 8 Design Decision 9's precedent for deferring a mechanization that is not yet an existing, known-to-matter rule for this specific harness.

### 9.2 User Story

As a developer running the Claude Code SDLC pipeline unattended, I want `verifier` to tell me plainly when code is present-but-unexercised rather than rounding that up to a pass, I want a failed verification to feed itself back into planning without me editing a file, I want the adversarial plan review to be a real, versioned, tool-restricted agent that also runs on plans `/develop-feature` produces autonomously, I want `code-reviewer` and `security-auditor` to stop blocking me on speculative or repeated findings while never staying silent on a CRITICAL one, and I want every implementation plan to start with a real end-to-end tracer and a write-surface declaration `/develop-feature` checks before it ever spawns two subagents that could collide on the same file — so that a run reporting `MERGE READY` means what it says.

### 9.3 Functional Requirements

#### FR-1: Verifier — Four Truth Verdicts (supersedes Section 1 FR-1.5's PASS/FAIL/WARN Overall line)

1. **FR-1.1:** `agents/verifier.md`'s Output Format section MUST replace its `### Overall: PASS / FAIL / WARN` line with exactly four mutually exclusive verdicts: `VERIFIED`, `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED`, `FAILED`, `UNCERTAIN`. Levels 1–4's own per-level PASS/FAIL/WARN/SKIPPED lines are unchanged — only the Overall line's vocabulary changes.
2. **FR-1.2 (precedence, evaluated in this fixed order):**
   - **FAILED** — any of Level 1, Level 2, or Level 3 report FAIL (per their existing PASS/FAIL definitions in `agents/verifier.md`, and per FR-4's BLOCKER tier for Level 2).
   - **UNCERTAIN** — none of the above, AND `verifier` could not reach a determination for at least one level (Level 1 reports `SKIPPED — cannot determine expected artifacts` because no plan or scratchpad was available; Level 3 cannot resolve a dynamic `import()`/`require()` call per its existing "SKIPPED — dynamic import" adaptation; **Level 4 itself reports `SKIPPED`** — e.g. every candidate data-flow path is behind a dynamic import, leaving Level 4 nothing it can even attempt to trace — **this case MUST be classified as UNCERTAIN, never as PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED: no specific gap exists for FR-10's replan loop to target, and feeding an unverifiable path into that loop as though some slice could close it would be incorrect**; or a finding is genuinely ambiguous under `verifier`'s static-analysis-only scope).
   - **VERIFIED** — none of the above, AND Level 4 confirms at least one real, non-hardcoded data path or an existing automated test exercises the feature's behavior at runtime (see FR-1.3).
   - **PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED** — none of the above (Levels 1–3 PASS, no UNCERTAIN condition applies) AND Level 4 finds no test or trace exercising the behavior. This is the default outcome for a feature that is present and correctly wired but has nothing demonstrating it runs correctly — it MUST NOT be reported as `VERIFIED` merely because nothing failed.
3. **FR-1.3 (what "exercised" means for Level 4, so VERIFIED vs. PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED is a checkable distinction, not a judgment call):** A data path counts as exercised when `verifier` finds at least one of: (a) an existing automated test (in a project test directory or `*.test.*`/`*.spec.*` file) that calls the new code path with non-trivial input and asserts on its output; (b) an E2E scenario file under `docs/use-cases/` or the project's E2E suite that names the specific flow; (c) a traced chain (route handler → service → data layer, or component → API call → state → render) where every link uses a real parameter or a real query result, with no hardcoded stand-in value at any link. Finding only that the chain is *wired* (imports resolve, the route is registered) without any of (a)–(c) is insufficient for VERIFIED and MUST be reported as a Level 4 gap contributing to PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED.
4. **FR-1.4:** `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md`'s Gate 6 row in its Output Format table (currently `| Goal-Backward Verification | PASS/FAIL/WARN | WARN = Level 4 advisory only |`) MUST be updated to render one of the four FR-1.2 verdicts verbatim in the Status column, replacing the PASS/FAIL/WARN vocabulary there.
5. **FR-1.5:** Gate 6's existing note in `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md` — "Note: Level 4 failures produce WARN, not FAIL — they are advisory and do not block merge." — MUST be replaced with wording reflecting Design Decision 1: a Level 4 gap does not by itself produce `FAILED`, but it does produce `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED` rather than `VERIFIED`, and per FR-10.4 that is not equivalent to a pass.

#### FR-2: Verifier — Machine-Readable Output

1. **FR-2.1:** `agents/verifier.md`'s `tools:` frontmatter MUST become exactly `["Read", "Glob", "Grep", "Write"]` — changed from `["Read", "Glob", "Grep"]` by adding `Write` only. `Edit` and `Bash` MUST NOT be added, now or by any later cleanup: `verifier` stays incapable of editing existing files and incapable of running its own commands, including `date -u` — see FR-2.7 for how it obtains a timestamp without one.
2. **FR-2.2 (deny-by-default wording, not a permission grant):** `agents/verifier.md`'s Constraints section MUST replace its unqualified "Read-only: you MUST NOT modify any files" line with a prohibition, not a scope grant: "`verifier` MUST NOT Write to any path other than `docs/verification/<feature-slug>.md`, and MUST NOT Edit any file." This phrasing — deny-by-default rather than "MAY Write only to X" — is a binding distinction, not cosmetic.
3. **FR-2.3:** At the end of every Gate 6 run, `verifier` MUST write `docs/verification/<feature-slug>.md` (the slug matching the feature branch/PRD-section slug already used for `docs/use-cases/<feature>_use_cases.md` and `docs/qa/<feature>_test_cases.md`), creating the `docs/verification/` directory on first write if it does not exist. This file is written on every verdict, including `VERIFIED` — not only on failure.
4. **FR-2.4:** The file MUST open with YAML frontmatter containing at minimum these fields:
   ```yaml
   ---
   feature: <feature-slug>
   verdict: VERIFIED | PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED | FAILED | UNCERTAIN
   passed: true | false
   gaps:
     - level: 1 | 2 | 3 | 4
       finding: <what is unverified — one sentence>
       location: <file:line or file path>
       verifies_with: <the specific test, trace, or manual step that would verify it>
   human_verification_required:
     - <one sentence per item requiring a human or a later automated run to confirm>
   generated_at: <ISO 8601 UTC timestamp — supplied verbatim by Gate 6's delegation prompt, FR-2.7>
   generated_at_note: <string — present only when generated_at is omitted per FR-2.7, explaining why>
   ---
   ```
   `gaps` and `human_verification_required` MUST both be present as arrays (empty `[]` when there is nothing to report — never omitted). `gaps` MUST list every individual finding that contributes to a `FAILED` or `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED` verdict — every Level 1–3 FAIL, every BLOCKER-tier and WARNING-tier Level 2 marker (FR-4), and every Level 4 unexercised-path finding — not a summary; this is the complete finding set FR-10's replan loop consumes. When `generated_at` is omitted per FR-2.7, the frontmatter MUST instead include `generated_at_note` explaining why no timestamp was supplied.
5. **FR-2.5:** Every `gaps` entry MUST carry all four fields (`level`, `finding`, `location`, `verifies_with`); an entry missing any of the four is malformed (FR-3.3's malformed-report handling applies identically here).
6. **FR-2.6:** The body of the file (below the frontmatter) MUST retain `agents/verifier.md`'s existing prose `### Verification Report` structure (Levels 1–4, findings with `file:line` references) unchanged in kind — the frontmatter is additive machine-readable structure, not a replacement for the human-readable report.
7. **FR-2.7 (Gate 6 supplies feature slug and timestamp — `verifier` MUST NOT invent either):** `verifier` has no `Bash` tool (FR-2.1) and cannot run `date -u` itself. `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md` Gate 6 MUST, before delegating to `verifier`, obtain the current UTC timestamp via the same `date -u +'%Y-%m-%d %H:%M'` command this file's own Finalization: Changelog Entry step already uses, and MUST state both the **feature slug** and this **timestamp** verbatim in the delegation prompt to `verifier`. `verifier` MUST use the supplied timestamp verbatim for `generated_at` and MUST NOT invent, estimate, or independently compute one. When `verifier` is invoked without a supplied timestamp (e.g. a direct, ad hoc invocation outside `/merge-ready`), it MUST omit `generated_at` and include `generated_at_note` instead (FR-2.4) — never hallucinate a value, extending `src/rules/changelog.md`'s never-invent-timestamps rule to verification reports.

#### FR-3: The `passed` Invariant

1. **FR-3.1:** `passed: true` is valid in `docs/verification/<feature>.md`'s frontmatter if and only if `human_verification_required` is an empty array. This is the mechanism that stops "I could not check this myself" from being silently reported as success.
2. **FR-3.2 (verdict-to-`passed`/`human_verification_required` mapping, MUST be followed exactly):**
   - `VERIFIED` → `passed: true`, `human_verification_required: []`.
   - `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED` → `passed: false`, `human_verification_required` MUST contain at least one entry describing what remains to be exercised.
   - `FAILED` → `passed: false`; `human_verification_required` MAY be empty (a `FAILED` verdict from a missing file or a BLOCKER marker needs no human judgment to resolve — it needs FR-10's replan loop).
   - `UNCERTAIN` → `passed: false`, `human_verification_required` MUST contain at least one entry describing what made the determination impossible.
3. **FR-3.3 (malformed-report handling):** A `docs/verification/<feature>.md` where `passed: true` and `human_verification_required` is non-empty — whether produced by a `verifier` defect or by manual editing — is malformed. `/merge-ready` Gate 6, when reading the report, MUST treat a malformed report identically to a `FAILED` verdict: the Status column reads `FAILED (malformed report: passed:true with non-empty human_verification_required)`, and the overall result MUST NOT be `MERGE READY` while this condition holds. This check MUST be performed by re-reading the file's frontmatter directly during Gate 6, not inferred from `verifier`'s own prose claim about its verdict.

#### FR-4: Anti-Pattern Severity Split (supersedes Section 1 FR-1.2's undifferentiated marker list)

1. **FR-4.1 (marker-to-severity table):**

   | Marker | Old tier (Section 1) | New tier (this feature) |
   |---|---|---|
   | `TBD` | not scanned (new) | BLOCKER, unless an issue reference is present on the same line (then WARNING) |
   | `FIXME` | undifferentiated FAIL | BLOCKER, unless an issue reference is present on the same line (then WARNING) |
   | `XXX` | undifferentiated FAIL | BLOCKER, unless an issue reference is present on the same line (then WARNING) |
   | `TODO` | undifferentiated FAIL | WARNING (unconditional) |
   | `HACK` | undifferentiated FAIL | WARNING (unconditional) |
   | `PLACEHOLDER` / `placeholder` | undifferentiated FAIL | WARNING (unconditional) |
   | `stub` | undifferentiated FAIL | BLOCKER (unconditional — signals absent implementation) |
   | `not implemented` | undifferentiated FAIL | BLOCKER (unconditional) |
   | `throw new Error('Not implemented')` | undifferentiated FAIL | BLOCKER (unconditional) |
   | `raise NotImplementedError` | undifferentiated FAIL | BLOCKER (unconditional) |
   | `pass  # TODO` | undifferentiated FAIL | BLOCKER (unconditional — see FR-4.4) |

2. **FR-4.2 (issue reference, defined):** An issue reference is a token on the **same line** as the marker matching one of: a bare `#<digits>` (e.g. `#123`); a project-key style token `<UPPERCASE-LETTERS>-<digits>` (e.g. `JIRA-456`, `GH-789`); or an issue/PR URL (`.../issues/<digits>` or `.../pull/<digits>`). A marker with no such token on the same line has no issue reference, regardless of whether one exists elsewhere in the file.
3. **FR-4.3:** Level 2's PASS/FAIL definition MUST change from "no markers found" to: **FAIL** when any BLOCKER-tier marker is found (per FR-4.1/FR-4.2); a file containing only WARNING-tier markers MUST still report Level 2 as **PASS**, with each WARNING-tier marker listed as a non-blocking finding and mirrored into `docs/verification/<feature>.md`'s `gaps` array (FR-2.4) at `level: 2`.
4. **FR-4.4 (exception — the `pass  # TODO` compound pattern):** Although the bare token `TODO` is WARNING-tier per FR-4.1, the specific compound pattern `pass  # TODO` (a Python function body consisting only of `pass` and a trailing `# TODO` comment) remains BLOCKER-tier unconditionally, because it denotes an empty implementation relying on the comment as its only content — the same class of finding as `stub` or `raise NotImplementedError`, not a deferred-cleanup marker on otherwise-complete code. `verifier` MUST check for this compound pattern before applying the bare-`TODO` WARNING rule.
5. **FR-4.5:** A BLOCKER-tier Level 2 finding contributes to the `FAILED` overall verdict per FR-1.2's precedence; a WARNING-tier finding does not change the overall verdict on its own and MUST NOT cause `FAILED`.

#### FR-5: Plan Critic Extraction — `agents/plan-critic.md`

1. **FR-5.1:** A new file `agents/plan-critic.md` MUST exist with frontmatter `name: plan-critic`, a `description`, a `tools` list, and a `model`. The `tools` list MUST NOT include `Write` — the agent critiques plans, it MUST NOT edit them. This requirement leaves the specific `model` tier unspecified; the implementation sets `model: opus`, matching the other three `opus`-tier agents (`architect`, `planner`, `security-auditor`) on the same "output cascades through the pipeline, mistakes aren't catchable by automated verification" rationale documented in `README.md`'s Model Tiers table. This is a planner judgment call, not a binding requirement of this FR, and is explicitly revisitable at F4's model-tier review.
2. **FR-5.2:** The prompt body MUST carry forward, in substance, every check currently in `src/claude.md` lines 102–166: Completeness, Slice Quality, File Path Verification (MANDATORY — Glob and Grep), Architecture & Security, Edge Cases & Testability, Scope Reduction Detection (with its full hedging-term list and its three "do NOT flag" exclusions), and Wave Assignment Validation (with its five sub-checks, including case-insensitive-filesystem awareness) — reformatted as agent-prompt sections (e.g. `## Process`, `## Checks`, `## Output Format`, `## Constraints`) rather than a "launch a subagent with this prompt" blockquote. No check MAY be dropped or weakened in the move. **Implementation note (intentional, non-weakening deviation — see AC-4):** the pre-migration prompt hardcoded the paths `.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `.claude/rules/` for the "read the project's CLAUDE.md and rules" step. Those exact paths do not exist in every project — this repository itself keeps them at `src/claude.md`/`src/rules/` — so `agents/plan-critic.md` generalizes that step to search wherever the project actually keeps its rules files. This widens what resolves; it drops no check and changes no check's meaning.
3. **FR-5.3 (FORCE stance):** The prompt MUST retain, verbatim or in clearly equivalent language, the current opening instruction: "Your job is to find problems in this plan, NOT to praise it" — plus an explicit closing instruction that a zero-finding result MUST be treated with skepticism ("Plans almost always have issues"), matching the existing prompt's closing line.
4. **FR-5.4 (verdict relabeling — literal mapping, no check content changes):** The finding scale changes from `CRITICAL|MAJOR|MINOR` to `BLOCKER|WARNING|INFO`, mapped 1:1: `CRITICAL → BLOCKER`, `MAJOR → WARNING`, `MINOR → INFO`. Every existing severity assignment in the moved checks (e.g., Section 2 FR-5.1's "shared file within a wave = CRITICAL," Section 1 FR-4.2's "MAJOR finding" for scope reduction) is carried forward under its new label with unchanged meaning.
5. **FR-5.5 (max 3 revision loops, then escalate — fix pass and re-invocation are gated independently):** The critique-and-fix cycle MUST run as follows: (1) invoke `plan-critic` (loop 1); (2) **the fix pass is gated on any BLOCKER-or-WARNING finding being present, not on a BLOCKER specifically:** whenever the loop just completed returns at least one BLOCKER or at least one WARNING finding, fix the plan file for every BLOCKER and every WARNING finding — this step is NOT conditional on a BLOCKER being present, since WARNING is where Scope Reduction Detection lands (FR-1's hedging-language check, carried forward per FR-5.2) and a hedging finding must be fixed, not merely noted; (3) **re-invocation is gated on a BLOCKER specifically:** re-invoke `plan-critic` against the revised plan (loop 2) only if the loop just completed found at least one BLOCKER finding — a loop that found WARNING findings only does not trigger a further loop, even though its WARNING findings were fixed per step 2; (4) repeat once more if needed (loop 3). If zero BLOCKER findings remain after any loop, proceed immediately — having already applied step 2's fixes (WARNING findings remaining, if deliberately left unfixed, are recorded per FR-5.6 with their justification, without a further loop). If a BLOCKER finding remains after loop 3, the workflow MUST escalate per Section 1 FR-2.4 (Rule 4 — present the remaining BLOCKER findings verbatim, the decision needed, and options) rather than proceeding — this applies identically whether the loop was invoked from the plan-mode stub (FR-5.6) or from `/bootstrap-feature` Step 5 (FR-5.9).
6. **FR-5.6 (delegation stub remains in `src/claude.md`):** `src/claude.md`'s "Plan Critic Pass" section retains its Step 2 (Incorporate Findings) and Step 3 (Append Review Notes) structure, updated only for BLOCKER/WARNING/INFO terminology (FR-5.4) and the loop mechanism (FR-5.5, replacing the current "Do NOT re-run the critic. One pass is sufficient." line). Step 1 ("Spawn Plan Critic") is rewritten from "Launch a `Plan` subagent with this prompt: [65-line blockquote]" to a one-line delegation: invoke the `plan-critic` agent (via the `Agent` tool, matching how `prd-writer`/`ba-analyst`/etc. are already invoked elsewhere in this pipeline) with the plan file path, per the FR-5.5 loop. Step 1 MUST additionally state the fail-visible fallback required when `plan-critic` is unresolvable (a memory-layer-only install with no plugin agents present — the same install gap `skills/develop-feature/SKILL.md`'s and `skills/bootstrap-feature/SKILL.md`'s existing Preflight: Memory Layer Check already warns about for the pipeline instruction itself): warn, naming `plan-critic` as unresolvable, and proceed to `ExitPlanMode` without a critique — it MUST NOT silently skip the critique pass with no notice at all.
7. **FR-5.7:** `agents/plan-critic.md` brings the agent count from 13 to 14 (see NFR-4, NFR-6).
8. **FR-5.8:** `src/claude.md`'s "Agency Roles" table MUST gain a row: `| Plan Critic | plan-critic | Adversarial plan review — BLOCKER/WARNING/INFO findings before implementation begins |`.
9. **FR-5.9 (wire the critic into `/bootstrap-feature`, closing the audited gap):** `skills/bootstrap-feature/SKILL.md` Step 5 ("Tech Lead — Implementation Planning") MUST gain a sub-step, after the planner produces the plan and before Step 6 (Git Setup): invoke `plan-critic` against the plan per the FR-5.5 loop. This is the first point at which a plan produced entirely by `/develop-feature` (no interactive plan-mode involved) is critiqued at all. `skills/bootstrap-feature/SKILL.md`'s Output Format MUST gain a "Plan Critique" block reporting the final verdict and BLOCKER/WARNING/INFO counts, alongside the existing "Architecture Review" block.
10. **FR-5.10 (three BLOCKER checks close the marker-omission gap):** `agents/plan-critic.md` MUST include three additional BLOCKER-tier checks: (i) the plan contains no slice marked `**Tracer:** yes`; (ii) the plan has `Wave:` fields and Wave 1 contains a slice not marked `**Tracer:** yes` (a non-tracer slice sharing, or occupying instead of, the tracer's required Wave 1 per FR-8.5); (iii) any wave's declared `Files (union)` column (FR-9.1) does not equal the actual union of its own slices' `Files:` lists. Because every freshly authored plan already passes through `plan-critic` (via FR-5.6's plan-mode stub or FR-5.9's `/bootstrap-feature` invocation), a planner that forgot the tracer marker is caught here as an authoring defect at critique time — a plan that genuinely predates this feature, and is never resubmitted to `plan-critic`, is untouched by these checks.
11. **FR-5.11 (the agent count must read 14 everywhere it is hardcoded, not just `README.md`):** Every live location that states the agent count as 13 MUST be updated to 14 alongside `agents/plan-critic.md` landing: `README.md`'s opening description (line 5, "13 specialized AI agents") and its "## The 13 Agents" heading (line 110); `install.sh`'s install banner (line 123, `agents/  13 specialized agents — ship in the plugin`); `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`'s `description` field (line 4, "...13 specialized agents..."); and `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`'s plugin-entry `description` field (line 14, the same string). **Named exceptions, MUST NOT be touched:** (a) `README.md` line 280 — "upgrading from v3.x removes the 13 agent files and 5 command files that older versions installed into `~/.claude/`" — is a historical fact about what v3.x shipped, not a live count, and stays `13` regardless of this feature's agent count; (b) `manifests/owned-files.txt`'s `legacy` section (lines 32/36 and the file enumeration at 37–49) — a v3.x **removal** manifest read by `install.sh --uninstall`/upgrade to delete files v3.x itself installed into `~/.claude/`. Its "13 agents" comment and its 13-entry file list are, like README line 280, a historical fact about what v3.x shipped, not a live count: adding `plan-critic.md` there would make the installer try to delete a plugin file v3.x never installed, and changing "13" to "14" in the comment would misstate history. This manifest stays untouched by this feature.

#### FR-6: Reviewer Confidence Filter — `code-reviewer` and `security-auditor`

1. **FR-6.1:** `agents/code-reviewer.md` and `agents/security-auditor.md` MUST report only findings the agent assesses at greater than 80% confidence of being real and actionable; a finding below that threshold MUST be omitted from the Output Format's findings list entirely — not merely deprioritized or footnoted. This threshold MUST be verified behaviorally, not numerically — there is no testable "exactly 80%" boundary against a model's self-assessed confidence: verification means seeding a genuinely ambiguous, low-confidence finding and confirming it is omitted, and separately seeding an unambiguous CRITICAL finding (FR-6.2) and confirming it is retained regardless.
2. **FR-6.2 (CRITICAL is never filtered):** Findings at CRITICAL severity MUST be reported regardless of the agent's confidence assessment. FR-6.1's threshold MUST NOT apply to CRITICAL-tier findings under any circumstance.
3. **FR-6.3 (`code-reviewer` gains severity tiers to support FR-6.2):** `agents/code-reviewer.md`'s Output Format, currently a flat `**Issues found**` list with no severity prefix, MUST gain a four-tier severity prefix per issue — `CRITICAL`, `HIGH`, `MEDIUM`, `LOW` — mirroring `agents/security-auditor.md`'s existing three-tier convention (plus `LOW`), so FR-6.2's never-filtered carve-out applies identically across both reviewer agents.
4. **FR-6.4 (consolidation):** Multiple findings sharing the same root cause across different locations MUST be consolidated into a single reported finding listing all affected locations, rather than one entry per location.
5. **FR-6.5 (skip unchanged code — applies only when a diff was actually supplied):** For a given `git diff`, `code-reviewer` and `security-auditor` MUST NOT report a finding in code outside the diff's changed hunks, unless that finding is CRITICAL severity (FR-6.6). Neither agent has a `Bash` tool, so neither can derive a diff on its own; this rule presupposes the delegation prompt supplied a diff, a changed-file list, or a branch range (the normal Gate 2/Gate 3 trigger). **When no diff-equivalent scope is supplied at all** (e.g. an ad hoc `Pre-review:` invocation outside Gate 2/3), this rule does not apply — the agent has no notion of "changed" and MUST scan the whole codebase as it did before this feature, rather than silently suppressing every non-CRITICAL finding for lack of a scope to compare against.
6. **FR-6.6:** A pre-existing CRITICAL finding immediately adjacent to, but outside, a changed hunk MUST still be reported — the diff-scoping rule in FR-6.5 carries the same CRITICAL exception as the confidence filter in FR-6.2.
7. **FR-6.7 (risk, recorded as required by design):** The confidence filter can suppress a real finding that happens to score below 80% — this is an accepted tradeoff, not an oversight. FR-6.2's CRITICAL carve-out is the primary mitigation; Gate 6 (`verifier`, unaffected by this filter, since it evaluates wiring and stub markers rather than review confidence) is a secondary backstop for wiring-level defects a filtered-out review finding might have caught first.

#### FR-7: Silent-Failure Hunting — absorbed into `code-reviewer`

1. **FR-7.1:** `agents/code-reviewer.md`'s Review Checklist MUST gain a "Silent Failures" category checking for: (a) empty catch blocks (`catch {}` / `catch (e) {}` with no body); (b) a `.catch()` handler that coerces the error into a benign default (e.g. `.catch(() => [])`, `.catch(() => null)`, `.catch(() => undefined)`) with no logging, rethrow, or user-facing signal; (c) a caught error whose only action is a `console.*`/logger call, with no rethrow, no propagation, and no caller-visible signal; (d) a promise chain with no `.catch()`/`try`-`catch` at all around an operation that can reject.
2. **FR-7.2 (severity):** A silent-failure finding is CRITICAL tier (per FR-6.3's new tiers) when the swallowed error occurs in a data-mutation or financial code path; HIGH tier otherwise.
3. **FR-7.3:** Silent-failure findings are exempt from FR-6.5's diff-scoping skip only when CRITICAL tier, per FR-6.6.

#### FR-8: Tracer-First Decomposition — `planner`

1. **FR-8.1:** `agents/planner.md`'s Output Format MUST require that Slice 1 of every plan is a vertical tracer: the thinnest possible end-to-end path exercising every architectural layer the feature touches (adapted to the project's actual layers per its `CLAUDE.md` — e.g. UI → API → service → data layer → response for a web app, or entry point → core logic → output for a library/CLI), carrying a real `Verify:` condition — not a types-only or scaffold-only slice.
2. **FR-8.2:** The per-slice format (Section 1 FR-3; Section 2 FR-1.6 added `Wave:` alongside it) MUST gain an explicit `**Tracer:** yes` marker on the tracer slice (present only there), so `/develop-feature` and `/implement-slice` can identify the tracer programmatically rather than assuming it is always Slice 1 by position.
3. **FR-8.3 (gating):** No slice other than the one marked `**Tracer:** yes` may be dispatched — by `/develop-feature` Phase 2 or by a standalone `/implement-slice` invocation — until the tracer slice's `Verify:` condition has been run and passed. `skills/implement-slice/SKILL.md` MUST gain a pre-flight check enforcing this: when invoked (standalone, or as a wave subagent) for a slice other than the tracer, and the plan's tracer slice has not yet passed its `Verify:` condition, it MUST refuse and instruct the caller to run the tracer slice first.
4. **FR-8.4 (tracer failure halts the phase):** When the tracer slice's `Verify:` condition still fails after the existing 3-retry budget (Section 1 FR-2.6) is exhausted, `/develop-feature` Phase 2 MUST halt before spawning any expansion-slice work (single or parallel) and report the blocker via the existing Rule 3/Rule 4 escalation path (Section 1 FR-2.3/FR-2.4). It MUST NOT proceed to Slice 2 with the tracer left broken.
5. **FR-8.5 (wave placement):** The tracer slice MUST occupy Wave 1 by itself; no other slice — regardless of file disjointness under Section 2 FR-1.2's overlap analysis — may share Wave 1 with the tracer. This overrides Section 2's disjointness-only wave-grouping heuristic for Wave 1 specifically, so FR-8.3's gate is enforced structurally by wave sequencing (Section 2 FR-2.4: `develop-feature` MUST wait for all subagents in wave N to complete before starting wave N+1), not only by the marker check in FR-8.3.
6. **FR-8.6 (no silent gate-vanish):** When `skills/develop-feature/SKILL.md` Phase 2 executes a plan containing no slice marked `**Tracer:** yes`, it MUST emit a one-line visible notice before proceeding to any slice: `tracer gate inactive — no **Tracer:** yes marker found; treating as pre-F3 plan.` NFR-3's legacy-plan fallback (no gate when no marker is present) MUST always be accompanied by this notice — the absence of a gate MUST be visible, never silent, matching Section 8's standing prohibition on a guard that looks installed but never runs.

#### FR-9: Write-Surface Declaration and Dispatch-Time Disjointness Check

1. **FR-9.1:** `agents/planner.md`'s wave summary table (Section 2 FR-1.4) MUST gain a `Files (union)` column: for each wave, the union of every slice's `Files:` entries in that wave.
2. **FR-9.2:** `skills/develop-feature/SKILL.md`'s Phase 2 "Multi-slice wave" dispatch step MUST, immediately before issuing any `Agent` tool call for a wave with 2+ pending slices, re-derive the `Files:` lists of exactly the slices about to be dispatched (read fresh from the plan file, not from memory) and check pairwise disjointness across those lists. A single-slice wave requires no check (nothing to conflict with).
3. **FR-9.3 (refusal):** When any file path appears in 2 or more of the about-to-be-dispatched slices' `Files:` lists, `/develop-feature` MUST refuse to dispatch that wave — issuing zero `Agent` tool calls for it — and report the specific conflicting file path together with the slice numbers that both declare it.
4. **FR-9.4 (always case-insensitive — no filesystem detection required):** The disjointness comparison MUST always be performed case-insensitively, regardless of the underlying filesystem, so that `src/Auth.ts` and `src/auth.ts` are always treated as the same file and flagged as a conflict. This is deliberately simpler than detecting filesystem case-sensitivity: on a case-sensitive filesystem where the two paths are genuinely different files, the comparison produces a false-positive conflict, which degrades safely into FR-9.6's replan path rather than requiring the orchestrating model to determine filesystem case-sensitivity itself.
5. **FR-9.5:** This check is a described procedure in `skills/develop-feature/SKILL.md`'s prose, performed by the orchestrating model itself before issuing the `Agent` tool calls — consistent with NFR-5's markdown-only scope for the agent/skill/memory layer, it is not a script (contrast FR-11, which does add a zero-dependency Node script, but only inside the CI zone NFR-5 does not govern).
6. **FR-9.6 (recovery path — never a dead end):** A refused wave MUST be treated as a Rule 3 auto-resolve (Section 1 FR-2.3) — re-invoke `planner` with the conflicting slice pair flagged, to reassign the offending slice to a later wave or split the file ownership — before falling back to a Rule 4 escalation (Section 1 FR-2.4) if the planner cannot resolve it automatically. This satisfies the autonomy contract's "never dead-end an unattended run."

#### FR-10: Gate 6 `--gaps` Replan Loop

1. **FR-10.1:** When Gate 6 reports `FAILED` or `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED` with a non-empty `gaps` array, `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md`'s existing Auto-Fix Protocol MUST, for Gate 6 specifically, feed the `gaps` array (the structured `{level, finding, location, verifies_with}` entries from `docs/verification/<feature>.md`'s frontmatter, FR-2.4) directly to the `planner` agent as input, rather than relying only on the orchestrating model's own reading of the report's prose body.
2. **FR-10.2 (`planner` returns slices; the orchestrator appends them):** `planner`'s `tools:` frontmatter (`Read, Glob, Grep, WebSearch, WebFetch`) has no `Write`/`Edit` and MUST NOT gain either — `planner` cannot append to the plan file itself. Given a `gaps` input, `planner` MUST instead **return** one or more replan slices in its output — using the standard `Files:`/`Changes:`/`Verify:`/`Done when:` fields (Section 1 FR-3) — each targeting a specific gap's `verifies_with` action (e.g. write the missing test, wire the missing import). The **orchestrator** MUST then append those returned slices to the plan file, append-only: every pre-existing slice MUST remain byte-identical, and the orchestrator MUST NOT modify any existing slice's fields while appending. This mirrors the established pattern in `skills/bootstrap-feature/SKILL.md` Step 7, where the orchestrator, not `planner`, performs the write for what `planner` returns. The orchestrator MUST NOT touch `docs/verification/<feature>.md` in the course of this append.
3. **FR-10.3:** Replan slices produced under FR-10.2 MUST be executed through the existing `/implement-slice` loop (TDD, commit, then Gate 6 reruns) and count against the same 3-attempt budget `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md`'s Auto-Fix Protocol already enforces per gate ("Repeat until all gates pass OR 3 fix attempts exhausted").
4. **FR-10.4:** The overall `MERGE READY` / `NOT MERGE READY` result MUST be `NOT MERGE READY` whenever Gate 6's verdict is `FAILED`, `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED`, or `UNCERTAIN` (including the malformed-report case, FR-3.3). Only a `VERIFIED` verdict with `passed: true` permits Gate 6's Status column to read as passing.
5. **FR-10.5 (persist the attempt counter — survive context compaction):** The Gate 6 `--gaps` loop's attempt count (bounded at 3 by FR-10.3) MUST be persisted in `.claude/scratchpad.md` — e.g. a `Gate 6 attempts: N/3` line under the feature's current status — written after each attempt and read back from the file, not from conversation memory, before deciding whether to retry. An in-memory-only count would silently reset to zero after a context compaction mid-loop, unbounding the retry loop; the scratchpad is the harness's existing durable-state mechanism for exactly this failure mode.
6. **FR-10.6 (mandatory re-review of replan commits — closes a reviewer-gate bypass):** The replan loop is the one place in the pipeline where the harness writes production code downstream of both reviewer gates — Gate 2 (Code Review) and Gate 3 (Security Audit) already ran, earlier, over the pre-replan diff, and by construction never see the replan slice(s)' own commits before Gate 6 reruns. Once the replan loop has committed any slice under FR-10.3, `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md` MUST re-run Gate 2 and Gate 3 over those specific commits before a subsequent `VERIFIED` from Gate 6 may permit `MERGE READY`. A `VERIFIED` verdict reached over unreviewed replan commits MUST NOT be treated as satisfying FR-10.4.

#### FR-11: CI Self-Verification — `scripts/ci/validate-verification-upgrade.js`

Only 24 of the 98 test cases in `docs/qa/verification-review-upgrade_test_cases.md` are automatable in CI today ("24/98 (≈24.5%) automatable in CI today," the QA document's own STATIC-vs-BEHAVIORAL total). Without a dedicated validator, this feature — whose entire purpose is preventing "present but unverified" from being reported as success — would itself ship almost entirely unverified. This is a required slice, not an optional hardening pass.

1. **FR-11.1:** A new zero-dependency Node validator `scripts/ci/validate-verification-upgrade.js` MUST exist, following the existing pattern every validator in `scripts/ci/` already uses (the shared `scripts/ci/lib/validate-core.js` helpers, one `core.run(...)` entry point). It MUST cover every one of the 24 STATIC-category test cases enumerated in `docs/qa/verification-review-upgrade_test_cases.md` as a machine-checked assertion, not merely the agent-frontmatter shape checks `scripts/ci/validate-agents.js` (Section 6 FR-5.2) already performs.
2. **FR-11.2 (minimum assertion set):** At minimum, the validator MUST assert: `agents/plan-critic.md` exists with `tools` excluding `Write` (FR-5.1); `agents/verifier.md`'s `tools` is exactly `Read, Glob, Grep, Write`, no `Edit`, no `Bash` (FR-2.1); `agents/verifier.md`'s Output Format section states the four verdict tokens `VERIFIED`, `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED`, `FAILED`, `UNCERTAIN` and no longer states the old three-verdict `PASS/FAIL/WARN` vocabulary (FR-1.1); `skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md` no longer contains the old "WARN = Level 4 advisory only" note (FR-1.5); the agent count of 14 is consistent across `README.md`, `install.sh`, `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, and `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (FR-5.11), excluding `README.md` line 280.
3. **FR-11.3 (seeded-bad fixtures — the validator must be provably capable of failing):** For every assertion in FR-11.2, `tests/fixtures/ci/verification-upgrade/*` MUST include at least one seeded-bad fixture the validator rejects — e.g. an `agents/plan-critic.md` fixture with `Write` present in `tools`; a `tools:` fixture for `verifier` that still includes `Bash`; an agent-count fixture where `install.sh` reads `13` while the others read `14`. A validator whose fixtures never fail proves nothing about the validator, only that it runs.
4. **FR-11.4 (anti-vacuity):** The validator MUST exit non-zero against an empty or absent tree (no `agents/plan-critic.md` present at all) — never silently pass because there was nothing to check. This mirrors `scripts/ci/validate-agents.js`'s existing `requireMinimum` pattern, applied to this validator's own checks.
5. **FR-11.5:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `validate-assets` job MUST invoke `node scripts/ci/validate-verification-upgrade.js` alongside the existing validators, in the same job as the rest of the harness's CI validation.

### 9.4 Non-Functional Requirements

1. **NFR-1 (autonomy contract — governing, inherited from the roadmap unchanged):** Every capability in this feature MUST (a) fire from an existing pipeline point — Gate 2, Gate 3, Gate 6, or an existing step of `/bootstrap-feature`/the plan-mode stub — never a new command a human must remember to run; (b) never dead-end an unattended run — a failure degrades to a warning (FR-4.3's WARNING tier, FR-6.7's accepted-risk framing) or escalates through the existing Section 1 FR-2 deviation rules (FR-5.5, FR-9.6, FR-10) rather than blocking without a recovery path; (c) treat manual invocation (e.g. running `/merge-ready gate-6` to rerun a single gate, per its existing `$gate` argument) as an escape hatch only, never the sole path to any of this feature's checks.
2. **NFR-2 (no new pipeline stages):** Every requirement in FR-1 through FR-10 lands inside an existing `/merge-ready` gate (2, 3, or 6) or an existing step of `/bootstrap-feature` (Step 5) or the plan-mode Plan Critic Pass stub in `src/claude.md`. This feature adds no new skill file under `skills/` and no new slash command. FR-11's CI validator is governed by this NFR too, on the same basis, not as an exception to it: CI runs automatically on push, not a pipeline step a human invokes, mirroring the precedent Section 6 NFR-4 already established for shipping CI validators without adding a step to `/develop-feature`, `/bootstrap-feature`, `/implement-slice`, or `/merge-ready`.
3. **NFR-3 (backward compatibility):**
   - A `docs/verification/<feature>.md` written before this feature ships (three-verdict PASS/FAIL/WARN, no frontmatter) is not retroactively rewritten by this feature; the four-verdict scheme and frontmatter apply to `verifier` runs from this feature forward. Gate 6 encountering an old-format report with no `verdict:` frontmatter field MUST treat it as `UNCERTAIN` (cannot determine a current-scheme verdict) and request a fresh `verifier` run, rather than erroring.
   - A plan with no `Wave:` fields (Section 2's existing fallback: one slice per wave, sequential) makes FR-8.5's Wave-1-alone rule vacuous (there is already only one slice per wave) and FR-9.2's disjointness check a no-op (nothing to compare within a wave of size 1) — both requirements degrade to their existing single-slice behavior automatically, with no special-casing required in the orchestration prose.
   - A plan with no slice carrying `**Tracer:** yes` (produced before this feature, or by a planner invocation that predates FR-8) MUST NOT be retroactively gated by FR-8.3 — the gate applies only when an explicit tracer marker is present. Its absence means the plan predates this feature and executes in its existing slice order with no tracer-gate, identical to pre-feature behavior — except that FR-8.6 now requires this fallback to be announced with a visible notice rather than occurring silently, and FR-5.10 ensures any *freshly authored* plan missing the marker is caught by `plan-critic` before it ever reaches this fallback.
4. **NFR-4 (agent budget):** This feature adds one agent (`agents/plan-critic.md`), bringing the total from 13 to 14, against the v4.0 hard budget of ≤16 agents (Section 6 NFR-5). Skill count and hook count are unaffected by this feature.
5. **NFR-5 (markdown-only — scoped to the agent/skill/memory layer; the CI zone is governed separately):** This constraint governs the agent/skill/memory layer only — `agents/*.md`, `skills/*/SKILL.md`, `src/claude.md` — which is what it exists to protect: none of those files, as modified by this feature, contains JavaScript, TypeScript, Python, or shell script. It does **not** extend to `scripts/ci/`. Per Section 7 NFR-1 (which extends Section 6 NFR-1 to three distinct Node contexts: `scripts/ci/` — fail-closed, zero-dependency Node, the same zone F1 and F2a already ship validators into; `install.sh` — barred from invoking `node` or `jq` entirely; and the hook runtime Section 7 introduces, which fails open), FR-11's zero-dependency Node addition inside `scripts/ci/` is expected and governed by that existing zone rule, not a violation of this NFR or a new exception invented for this feature. Validation of the new agent runs through the existing `scripts/ci/validate-agents.js` unmodified — its `MIN_AGENTS` check is a floor (13), not an equality check, by its own header comment, so 14 agent files pass without editing the validator.
6. **NFR-6 (PRD §4 conflict — known, accepted, not an oversight):** Section 4 NFR-4 states "No new agents are added. The total agent count remains at 13." FR-5.1/FR-5.7 of this feature directly violate that constraint by adding `agents/plan-critic.md`. This conflict is recorded here, not resolved here: Section 4 itself is not edited by this feature, and its revision (along with NFR-1, NFR-5, and FR-1.5's flat lesson-entry format, per the roadmap) is deferred to F5, which the roadmap's execution order places after both F3 and F4.

### 9.5 Acceptance Criteria

1. **AC-1:** A seeded feature slice that is present, stub-free, and correctly wired (Levels 1–3 PASS) but has no test or trace exercising its runtime behavior produces `docs/verification/<feature>.md` with `verdict: PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED` and `passed: false`; `/merge-ready`'s Gate 6 row in its output table shows `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED`, not `PASS` or `VERIFIED`, and the overall result is `NOT MERGE READY` (FR-1.2, FR-10.4).
2. **AC-2:** Gate 6 failing with a non-empty `gaps` array causes `planner` (read-only, no `Write`/`Edit`) to be invoked with that array and to return at least one replan slice, which the **orchestrator** then appends to the plan file (FR-10.1, FR-10.2) — verifiable as: the plan file's slice count strictly increases, every pre-existing slice is byte-identical before and after (diffable), and `docs/verification/<feature>.md` receives zero writes in the course of the append.
3. **AC-3:** A hand-crafted `docs/verification/<feature>.md` with `passed: true` and a non-empty `human_verification_required` array causes Gate 6 to report `FAILED (malformed report...)`, not `PASS`, when `/merge-ready gate-6` is rerun against it (FR-3.3).
4. **AC-4:** Running `agents/plan-critic.md` against a fixture plan file containing a known injected defect (e.g. a wave with two slices sharing a file, or a slice with an untestable `Done when:`) reports the finding at BLOCKER or WARNING severity as appropriate, at the same or better count as running the pre-migration inlined prompt (`src/claude.md` lines 102–166, captured before migration) against the identical fixture — verifiable by running both against the same input and diffing FINDINGS output (FR-5.2). This comparison is of finding *coverage*, not of literal prompt text: FR-5.2's path generalization (hardcoded `.claude/CLAUDE.md`/`.claude/rules/` → "wherever the project keeps them") is an intentional, non-weakening deviation from the captured pre-migration prompt, and does not itself constitute a coverage gap for this AC to flag.
5. **AC-5:** A plan with a wave containing 2+ slices whose `Files:` lists share a path is refused by `/develop-feature`'s Phase 2 dispatch step before any `Agent` tool call is issued for that wave; the refusal names the specific shared path and both slice numbers (FR-9.2, FR-9.3) — verifiable by constructing such a plan and confirming zero `Agent` invocations for the conflicting wave plus the named-conflict message.
6. **AC-6:** A tracer slice (marked `**Tracer:** yes`) whose `Verify:` command still fails after the existing 3-retry budget is exhausted halts `/develop-feature` Phase 2 before any Slice 2+ work begins — verifiable by seeding a failing tracer and confirming no commit exists for any expansion slice (FR-8.3, FR-8.4).
7. **AC-7:** `node scripts/ci/validate-agents.js` exits 0 against a repository state with 14 files under `agents/`, including a well-formed `plan-critic.md` (frontmatter `name: plan-critic`, non-empty `description`, valid `tools`, valid `model`); the same command exits non-zero when `agents/plan-critic.md` is missing a required frontmatter field, has a `name` not matching its filename, or lists an invalid `model` value (NFR-5).
8. **AC-8:** `agents/plan-critic.md`'s frontmatter `tools` list, read directly, does not contain `Write` (FR-5.1) — verifiable by reading the file.
9. **AC-9:** A seeded diff containing an empty `catch {}` block in a data-mutation code path is reported by `code-reviewer` as a CRITICAL-tier finding regardless of the 80% confidence threshold (FR-6.2, FR-7.1, FR-7.2) — verifiable by seeding the diff and confirming the finding appears in the Output Format's Issues list.
10. **AC-10:** A seeded diff containing 5 structurally identical missing-null-check findings across 5 files produces exactly one consolidated finding listing all 5 locations from `code-reviewer`, not 5 separate entries (FR-6.4).
11. **AC-11:** A production file containing `TBD` with no issue reference on the same line is reported as BLOCKER by Level 2 and contributes to an overall `FAILED` verdict; the identical file with `TBD(#42)` on the same line is reported as WARNING and does not, by itself, force `FAILED` (FR-4.1, FR-4.2, FR-4.3) — verifiable by seeding both variants and comparing Level 2 output and the overall verdict.
12. **AC-12:** A production file containing the compound pattern `pass  # TODO` as a function body is reported as BLOCKER, not WARNING, despite containing the bare token `TODO` (FR-4.4) — verifiable by seeding the pattern and confirming Level 2's tier assignment.
13. **AC-13:** `skills/bootstrap-feature/SKILL.md` Step 5 invokes `plan-critic` after the planner produces a plan and before Step 6 (Git Setup), verifiable by reading the file and confirming the invocation is present and precedes the Git Setup step (FR-5.9).
14. **AC-14:** `agents/verifier.md`'s frontmatter `tools` list is exactly `Read, Glob, Grep, Write` (no `Edit`, no `Bash`), and its Constraints section states the deny-by-default prohibition (FR-2.2) rather than an unqualified read-only statement or a permission grant — verifiable by reading the file (FR-2.1, FR-2.2).
15. **AC-15:** Gate 6's delegation prompt to `verifier` includes a UTC timestamp obtained via `date -u` and the feature slug; the resulting `docs/verification/<feature>.md`'s `generated_at` field matches that exact value verbatim. A direct, ad hoc invocation of `verifier` with no timestamp supplied produces a report with `generated_at` omitted and `generated_at_note` present, never an invented timestamp — verifiable by seeding both invocation paths and inspecting the frontmatter (FR-2.7).
16. **AC-16:** A plan whose tracer slice has not yet passed its `Verify:` condition causes a direct `/implement-slice` invocation targeting a non-tracer slice to refuse, per `skills/implement-slice/SKILL.md`'s pre-flight check — verifiable by invoking `/implement-slice` on Slice 2 before Slice 1 (the tracer) has passed, and confirming refusal (FR-8.3).
17. **AC-17:** The agent count reads 14 consistently across every live-count location — `README.md`'s opening description (line 5) and "## The 13 Agents" heading (line 110, retitled), `install.sh`'s install banner (line 123), `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`'s `description` field (line 4), and `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`'s plugin `description` field (line 14) — with `plan-critic` listed among the agents where the surrounding text enumerates them. `README.md` line 280's historical statement ("upgrading from v3.x removes the 13 agent files... that older versions installed") is unchanged, still reading `13` (FR-5.11) — verifiable by reading all five files.
18. **AC-18:** `agents/plan-critic.md` reports BLOCKER on each of three independent fixtures: (i) a plan with no `**Tracer:** yes` slice; (ii) a waved plan whose Wave 1 contains a non-tracer slice; (iii) a wave whose `Files (union)` column omits a file present in one of its slices' `Files:` lists — verifiable by running `plan-critic` against each fixture independently (FR-5.10).
19. **AC-19:** `/develop-feature`, run against a plan with no `**Tracer:** yes` slice, prints the exact notice `tracer gate inactive — no **Tracer:** yes marker found; treating as pre-F3 plan` before Phase 2 proceeds to any slice — verifiable by seeding such a plan and inspecting the transcript (FR-8.6).
20. **AC-20:** During a seeded Gate 6 `--gaps` loop spanning multiple attempts, `.claude/scratchpad.md` shows the attempt counter incrementing after each attempt (e.g. `Gate 6 attempts: 1/3`, then `2/3`) — verifiable by inspecting the scratchpad after each attempt, confirming the count is read from the file rather than re-derived (FR-10.5).
21. **AC-21:** A seeded feature where every candidate data-flow path is behind a dynamic import (Level 4 reports `SKIPPED`) with Levels 1–3 PASS produces verdict `UNCERTAIN`, not `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED`, with `human_verification_required` non-empty describing the undeterminable path — verifiable by seeding the dynamic-import case and inspecting the frontmatter (FR-1.2).
22. **AC-22:** `agents/planner.md`'s frontmatter `tools` list is unchanged by this feature (`Read, Glob, Grep, WebSearch, WebFetch`, no `Write`/`Edit`) — verifiable by reading the file and confirming the Gate 6 replan loop's plan-file mutation is attributable only to the orchestrator's own tool calls, never to a `planner` invocation (FR-10.2).
23. **AC-23:** In a simulated memory-layer-only install (plugin agents unresolvable), the plan-mode Plan Critic Pass stub in `src/claude.md` emits a visible warning naming `plan-critic` as unresolvable and proceeds to `ExitPlanMode` without blocking — it does not skip the critique step with no notice at all — verifiable by simulating an unresolvable `plan-critic` and inspecting the output (FR-5.6).
24. **AC-24:** `node scripts/ci/validate-verification-upgrade.js` exits 0 against the real, fully-implemented tree; exits non-zero against each seeded-bad fixture in `tests/fixtures/ci/verification-upgrade/*` (FR-11.3); and exits non-zero against an empty/absent tree with no `agents/plan-critic.md` present (FR-11.4, anti-vacuity) — a validator that only ever passes proves nothing.

### 9.6 Affected Components

#### New Files

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `agents/plan-critic.md` | Extracted, versioned Plan Critic — Completeness, Slice Quality, File Path Verification, Architecture & Security, Edge Cases & Testability, Scope Reduction Detection, Wave Assignment Validation checks, BLOCKER/WARNING/INFO findings, no `Write` tool (FR-5) |
| `docs/verification/<feature>.md` (generated per feature, one per `/merge-ready` run) | Machine-readable verification report — YAML frontmatter (`feature`, `verdict`, `passed`, `gaps`, `human_verification_required`, `generated_at`) plus the existing prose report body (FR-2) |
| `scripts/ci/validate-verification-upgrade.js` | Zero-dependency Node CI validator covering the 24 STATIC-category test cases from `docs/qa/verification-review-upgrade_test_cases.md` that are automatable without a live agent run (FR-11) |
| `tests/fixtures/ci/verification-upgrade/*` | Seeded-bad fixtures proving the validator can fail, plus the anti-vacuity empty-tree case (FR-11.3, FR-11.4) |

#### Modified Files

| File | Changes | Related Requirements |
|------|---------|---------------------|
| `agents/verifier.md` | Replace `Overall: PASS/FAIL/WARN` with the four verdicts and their precedence (including the Level-4-`SKIPPED`→`UNCERTAIN` pin); set `tools:` to exactly `Read, Glob, Grep, Write` (no `Edit`, no `Bash`); rewrite the Constraints "Read-only" line as deny-by-default wording; add machine-readable frontmatter writing, including verbatim (never invented) `generated_at`/`generated_at_note` handling; split Level 2's marker list into BLOCKER/WARNING tiers with the `pass  # TODO` exception | FR-1, FR-2, FR-4 |
| `agents/code-reviewer.md` | Add CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW severity tiers to the Output Format; add the 80% confidence filter with a CRITICAL carve-out; add consolidation; add diff-scoping with a CRITICAL carve-out; add the "Silent Failures" checklist category | FR-6, FR-7 |
| `agents/security-auditor.md` | Add the 80% confidence filter with a CRITICAL carve-out (its existing CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM tiers are unchanged); add consolidation; add diff-scoping with a CRITICAL carve-out | FR-6 |

Line 280's historical "13 agent files... that older versions installed" is NOT touched | FR-5.7, FR-5.11, NFR-4 |

### 9.7 UI Changes

Not applicable. This project is a collection of markdown prompt files with no user interface.

### 9.8 Schema Changes

Not applicable. This project has no database. `docs/verification/<feature>.md`'s YAML frontmatter (FR-2.4) is a per-feature document, not a schema in any persistence layer.

### 9.9 Affected Endpoints

Not applicable. This project has no API.

### 9.10 Risks and Dependencies

1. **Risk: A `--gaps` replan loop that never converges.** A gap whose `verifies_with` action cannot actually be satisfied (e.g. it names a test the project's stack cannot express) would cycle the planner and `/implement-slice` indefinitely. Mitigation: FR-10.3 binds replan slices to the same 3-attempt-per-gate budget the Auto-Fix Protocol already enforces; exhausting it reports `NOT MERGE READY` with specific blockers, per the existing protocol, rather than looping unbounded.
2. **Risk: `PRESENT_BEHAVIOR_UNVERIFIED` becomes noise if Level 4's "exercised" bar (FR-1.3) is set too strictly**, flagging real, adequately-tested features as unverified and triggering unnecessary replan loops. Mitigation: FR-1.3 defines three independent ways to satisfy "exercised" (an existing test, a named E2E scenario, or a fully-real traced chain) rather than requiring all three, and FR-10's replan loop degrades gracefully (adds a slice, does not block indefinitely per Risk 1's mitigation).
3. **Risk: The Plan Critic's 3-loop-then-escalate mechanism (FR-5.5) could make `/bootstrap-feature` (FR-5.9) noticeably slower for large plans**, since each loop is a full agent invocation. Mitigation: the loop terminates as soon as zero BLOCKER findings remain (commonly loop 1 or 2, not always 3); this is an accepted latency cost for the reliability gain, consistent with the roadmap's stated priority order (reliability first).
4. **Risk: FR-6's confidence filter and diff-scoping could suppress a real, non-CRITICAL finding that a human reviewer would have caught.** Recorded explicitly per FR-6.7: this is an accepted tradeoff for fewer false blocks on unattended runs, mitigated by the CRITICAL-never-filtered carve-out (FR-6.2, FR-6.6) and by Gate 6's independent, confidence-filter-unaffected wiring checks as a partial backstop.
5. **Risk: FR-8.5's Wave-1-alone rule could serialize work that was previously parallelizable**, since a slice that could have shared Wave 1 with the tracer under pure file-disjointness analysis is now pushed to Wave 2. Mitigation: this is a deliberate reliability-over-speed tradeoff scoped to Wave 1 only — Waves 2+ retain Section 2's existing disjointness-based parallelism unchanged.
6. **Risk: `docs/verification/<feature>.md` accumulates one file per feature indefinitely**, similar in kind to the growth risk Section 4 documented for `lessons.md`. Mitigation: out of scope for this feature — each file is small (one report per feature) and append-only across features, not append-only within one file; no consolidation mechanism is proposed here, and this is flagged as a standing note for a future feature if the directory's size becomes a practical concern.
7. **Dependency: Section 1 (`verifier`'s four levels, the deviation rules) must exist first.** FR-1 through FR-4 modify `verifier`'s existing Level 1–4 checks and Overall-verdict line; FR-5.5, FR-8.4, FR-9.6, and FR-10.3 all resolve into Section 1's existing four deviation rules and 3-retry budget. Section 1 is marked `[SHIPPED]`, so this dependency is satisfied.
8. **Dependency: Section 2 (wave assignment, the Plan Critic's Wave Assignment Validation) must exist first.** FR-8.5 and FR-9 extend Section 2's existing wave-assignment algorithm and wave summary table; FR-5.2 carries Section 2 FR-5's checks forward into `agents/plan-critic.md` unchanged. Section 2 is marked `[SHIPPED]`, so this dependency is satisfied.
9. **Dependency: Section 6 (agent budget, CI validator) must exist first.** `scripts/ci/validate-agents.js` (AC-7) is a Section 6 deliverable; this feature's agent-count acceptance criteria depend on it existing and its `MIN_AGENTS` floor behavior being as documented. Section 6 is marked `[DRAFT]` — per the roadmap's stated execution order (F1 → F2a → F2b, then F3/F4 in either order), F1 (Section 6) lands before F3.
10. **Dependency: Section 4 NFR-4 revision is deferred to F5, not this feature.** This feature's agent count (14) conflicts with Section 4 NFR-4's stated 13 (NFR-6). This PRD section does not edit Section 4; the roadmap places that revision in F5, after both F3 and F4. Until F5 lands, Section 4 NFR-4 and this section's NFR-6 remain in documented, acknowledged conflict — a reader consulting Section 4 alone would not see this note, the same gap Section 6's Related line flagged for its own NFR-1 conflict with Section 4.
11. **Dependency: Mandatory `security-auditor` pre-review — the `--gaps` replan loop (FR-10).** Feeding `verifier`-derived findings directly into `planner`, which then produces new slices executed without human review of the plan-file diff, is a structural change to how much autonomy the pipeline exercises without a human reading intermediate output. This slice REQUIRES `security-auditor` review before merge, scheduled as a pre-review during `/bootstrap-feature`, to confirm the loop cannot be induced (via a crafted `verifies_with` string) into producing a slice that weakens a security control under the guise of "closing a verification gap."
12. **Dependency: Mandatory `security-auditor` pre-review — `agents/verifier.md`'s new `Write` grant (FR-2.1).** This is the first time `verifier` gains any write capability since Section 1 created it as read-only. This slice REQUIRES `security-auditor` review before merge to confirm the deny-by-default wording in FR-2.2 is unambiguous and that no other instruction in the agent's prompt could be read as license to write elsewhere.
13. **Note: architect review incorporated as binding constraints, not assumed.** The architect returned PASS on this section with binding corrections, verified directly against `agents/verifier.md`'s and `agents/planner.md`'s actual `tools:` frontmatter: FR-2 originally implied `verifier` could self-obtain a timestamp it has no `Bash` tool to produce (fixed by FR-2.7 — Gate 6 supplies it); FR-10.2 originally implied `planner` could append to the plan file despite having no `Write`/`Edit` tool (fixed — the orchestrator appends what `planner` returns). Both are recorded here so the next reader sees PRD-vs-agent-capability agreement was actively checked, not assumed to hold because the PRD was written first — the same discipline Section 6 Risk 12 established for this document.
14. **Deferred: a generic agent-write-surface hook (keyed on `agent_type`) is the correct future mechanization of FR-2.2's deny-by-default `Write` scoping, explicitly not built here.** Today, `verifier`'s single-path `Write` restriction (FR-2.2) is enforced only by prompt wording, the same class of gap Section 8's blocking guards exist to close for other prose rules. A hook that mechanically denies any agent's `Write`/`Edit` call outside its own declared write surface, keyed on an `agent_type` indicator, would close this gap generally rather than one agent at a time. This is recorded as an F5-or-later candidate, consistent with Section 8 Design Decision 9's precedent for deferring a mechanization until it is an existing, known-to-matter rule rather than a hypothesis — it is explicitly not in scope for this feature.
15. **Note: planner-identified gap between this section's original NFR-5 scope and a required CI-validator slice, reconciled via Section 7's existing zone rule, not invented policy.** The implementation plan requires `scripts/ci/validate-verification-upgrade.js` (FR-11) because only 24 of 98 test cases in `docs/qa/verification-review-upgrade_test_cases.md` are automatable in CI without it — a feature whose entire purpose is preventing "present but unverified" from being reported as success would otherwise ship almost entirely unverified itself, the precise defect it exists to prevent. NFR-5 and §9.6's Unchanged Files table originally, and incorrectly, implied `scripts/ci/` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` were untouched. Both are corrected here to state that NFR-5's markdown-only constraint governs the agent/skill/memory layer only, per Section 7 NFR-1's already-shipped three-Node-context framing — the same reconciliation discipline Section 6 Risk 12 established for this document, applied again here rather than assumed to still hold.
16. **Note: the agent-count surface is wider than `README.md` alone (FR-5.11).** Three additional live locations hardcode "13 specialized agents" and would go false the moment `agents/plan-critic.md` lands if left unamended: `install.sh` (line 123), `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (line 4), and `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (line 14). `README.md` line 280 is a deliberate, named exception — a historical fact about what v3.x shipped — and MUST NOT be changed by any slice implementing FR-5.11, so a future cleanup pass does not "fix" it incorrectly.
