## 10. Adaptive Tier Routing and Model Routing

**Status:** [DRAFT]
**Date:** 2026-08-16
**Priority:** High
**Related:** Section 3 (`[SUPERSEDED]`, already naming this feature as its replacement — 10.1 below states precisely which of its FRs this feature fulfils and which it discards; Section 3's original three-opus rationale is directly revised by FR-8.3); Section 1 (FR-2, the four deviation rules — FR-2 below redirects Rule 4's default behavior for a `quick`-tier run without altering Rule 4's definition; Rules 1–3 apply unchanged inside every tier); Section 2 (wave/plan mechanics — a `quick`-tier plan is a degenerate single-slice, single-wave case of the existing `## Plan` format, not a new one); Section 5 (changelog automation — every tier's changelog write reuses the existing idempotency-guarded procedure verbatim; FR-3.4 and FR-4.5 name it explicitly rather than defining a second one); Section 6 (NFR-5, the v4.0 asset budget this feature's own NFR-5 below counts against; `scripts/ci/lib/validate-core.js`'s `--expect-failure`/`Validator`/`core.run` pattern, verified present at HEAD, is what FR-10's new validator is built on); Section 7 (NFR-1's three-Node-zone framing — `scripts/ci/` fail-closed, `install.sh` barred from Node/jq entirely, hooks fail-open through `run-hook.js` — extended here by NFR-4 with a fourth case for the statusline, which is dispatched by neither `install.sh` nor `run-hook.js`); Section 8 (`pre:bash:git-guard` and `stop:changelog-guard`, verified present at HEAD in `hooks/hooks.json`, already apply unmodified to every `fast`/`quick`-tier commit and changelog write with no tier-awareness required, since git hygiene and changelog shape are orthogonal to documentation tier; Design Decision 9's and Risk 4's precedent for deferring a hypothesis-only mechanization is the basis for 10.10 Risk 1's disposition of this feature's own known enforcement gap); Section 9 (`agents/planner.md`'s existing Replan Contract and Conflict Recovery Contract narrow-invocation-mode pattern, verified present at HEAD, is extended here with a third, Quick-Tier Contract mode, FR-4.1; `agents/plan-critic.md`'s model tier, left as "a planner judgment call... explicitly revisitable at F4's model-tier review" by Section 9 FR-5.1, is settled here by FR-8.1/FR-8.3; Gate 6's four-verdict scheme and `docs/verification/*` are unmodified — they simply never run inside `fast`/`quick` tier, per FR-4.7).

### 10.1 Description

The harness has one gear. A one-line typo fix and a new API endpoint both pass through PRD → use cases → architecture review → QA → 5–9 slices → 9 quality gates, or the request bypasses the pipeline entirely — and the bypassed path has no guards, no changelog, and no record. Developers bypass it. That is how autonomy actually dies: not through a missing capability, but through a capability so expensive to invoke correctly that skipping it becomes the rational choice. The fix is automatic triage, not a new command a human has to remember.

Separately, and independently verifiable at HEAD: `agents/*.md`'s `model:` values are hardcoded at authoring time (4 files `opus`, 10 files `sonnet` — confirmed by direct grep against all 14 files during this section's drafting), Claude Code reads a subagent's model from that frontmatter field and nothing else, no runtime config can override it, and deleting the field yields *inherit* for that agent, which is not tiering, it is simply not having a policy. Section 3 recorded the evidence for why this matters: three installed copies of this repository's agents, in a live `~/.claude/agents/` directory, had been hand-edited to `model: fable` while the repository itself had no mechanism to know. Section 3's own fix — hardcode a better static split and ask contributors to hand-edit frontmatter, then reinstall, when they disagree — does not close that gap; it is the same class of mechanism that produced the gap in the first place. This feature replaces it with a rewrite-at-install-time mechanism plus a CI check that can actually tell drift from policy.

**Why triage first, not model routing first:** the roadmap groups these two changes into one feature because they share a root cause — the harness applies one fixed policy (one pipeline depth, one model per agent) to every request, with no mechanism to right-size either one to what the specific request actually needs, and no mechanism to catch it when the applied policy silently stops matching the intended one.

**Design Decisions:**

1. **Escalation redirects; it never stops, except at the ceiling.** The autonomy contract (Section 6, restated in NFR-1 below) forbids a new capability from dead-ending an unattended run. A naive triage design would have a `fast`-tier run that turns out to be bigger than expected simply stop and ask — which is exactly the "one gear or bypass" failure this feature exists to fix, relocated one level down. FR-2 instead redirects: `fast` escalates to `quick`, `quick` escalates to `full` (even overriding Rule 4's own default "stop and ask" behavior, FR-2.2), and only `full` — which already has Rule 4's existing, autonomy-contract-compliant stop-and-ask behavior — is a legitimate stopping point.
2. **Downgrades are impossible by construction, not merely discouraged.** An automatic *downgrade* is a different and more dangerous failure mode than a failure to escalate: it is the mechanism by which a feature that legitimately needed full documentation would quietly lose it, mid-run, with nobody having asked for that. FR-2.6 states this as its own numbered requirement for exactly that reason — it is not a corollary of "escalation is one-way," it is the property that makes one-way escalation safe to build at all.
3. **Quick tier reuses the plan file this harness already has.** `.claude/scratchpad.md`'s `## Plan` section, and `/implement-slice`'s ability to read a single un-waved slice from it, already exist and already work exactly the way a "one subagent, one plan, TDD" tier needs. FR-4 introduces no second plan-file format — a `quick`-tier plan is one slice written into the same section a `full`-tier plan uses, which is what makes FR-4.4's one-line pre-flight bypass sufficient rather than requiring a parallel implementation path through `/implement-slice`.
4. **`planner`'s existing unconditional "read `docs/PRD.md`" is the actual unbounded-growth defect, verified directly against the shipped file.** `agents/planner.md`'s Process step 1 reads, verbatim, "Read `docs/PRD.md` — feature requirements and acceptance criteria" — the whole file, every time, with no section scoping. Sections 8 and 9 alone are several hundred lines each; this is not a hypothetical future problem, it is the file this PRD section is itself being appended to. FR-12 does not merely add an index alongside the existing behavior — FR-12.3 rewrites the unconditional whole-file read into a scoped one, and FR-12.4 adds the bounded index consultation for prior-feature context specifically, because adding a digest without removing the whole-file read would still leave the original defect fully intact.
5. **The model-profile tables are not a fresh design — they are Section 9's own deferred decision, made concrete.** Section 9 FR-5.1 shipped `agents/plan-critic.md` at `model: opus` "matching the other three opus-tier agents... This is a planner judgment call, not a binding requirement of this FR, and is explicitly revisitable at F4's model-tier review." FR-8 is that review. It revises two of Section 3's original three opus roles (`architect`, `planner`) under the `balanced`/`budget` profiles specifically because two backstops that did not exist when Section 3 was drafted — `plan-critic`'s adversarial review and Gate 6's `--gaps` replan loop, both Section 9 — now exist to catch what an opus-only policy was originally the sole defense against. `security-auditor` is not revised under any profile, because no equivalent backstop exists for a missed vulnerability.
6. **Two independently hand-maintained copies of the same table is a real, named risk, so the CI check verifies both directions.** `install.sh` cannot read a JS file without invoking `node` (forbidden, NFR-4), so the profile table exists once in shell (`install.sh`) and once in JS (`scripts/ci/lib/model-profiles.js`). Rather than accept this as an unmechanized maintenance risk — the posture Section 8 Risk 5 explicitly accepted for `git-guard`'s regex vs. `src/rules/git.md`'s prose — FR-10.3 has the CI validator parse `install.sh`'s table as text (never execute it) and assert the two agree, closing the gap a text-only PRD note would leave open.
7. **`inherit` is written as a literal value, never a deleted field.** The roadmap's own framing — "deleting `model:` yields *inherit* for every agent, which is not tiering" — describes the *accidental* version of this outcome: an agent silently, unrecordedly free-riding on host inheritance because nobody is managing the field. FR-7.3 makes the *deliberate* version of the same outcome distinguishable from that failure mode by keeping the field present and schema-valid (`scripts/ci/validate-agents.js`'s existing `REQUIRED_FIELDS` check, which this feature does not weaken) while still achieving the actual runtime behav.
8. **The statusline is not a hook, and nothing else in this feature depends on it running.** The roadmap's Risk 8 documents that hook stdin does not carry context-window usage — only the statusline's own JSON does, delivered to a mechanism separate from `hooks/lib/run-hook.js` entirely. Rather than build a side-channel to smuggle that data into the hook system (the roadmap explicitly declines this, citing GSD's `/tmp` side-channel as a pattern not being copied here), FR-13 treats the statusline as a fourth, independent Node-execution context (NFR-4) with its own fail-open-by-analogy contract (FR-13.5) and explicitly no reverse dependency (FR-13.6) — nothing in this feature, or planned by the roadmap, reads statusline output back into the pipeline.
9. **No new hook is added, and the one enforcement gap this creates is named, not hidden.** FR-2.1's fast→quick file-count/sensitive-path trigger, and FR-2.2(b)'s equivalent mid-quick-run trigger, have no mechanical enforcement — Section 8's guards do not check tier against Edit/Write call count or path, and this feature does not add one (the hook budget realistically has 3 slots of headroom left, one of which Section 8 Design Decision 9 already earmarked for a future `pre:edit:gateguard`). This is recorded plainly in 10.10 Risk 1, with a named future candidate, rather than left for a reader to discover that no guard actually checks it.

**§3 Supersession Ledger — what this feature fulfils and what it discards, stated precisely:**

| §3 requirement | Disposition under this feature |
|---|---|
| FR-1 / FR-2 (static hardcoded `model: sonnet`/`model: opus` per file, fixed at authoring time) | **Discarded as a mechanism.** Replaced by FR-7/FR-8's profile-driven rewrite. The underlying intent — right-size cost to task — is fulfilled, not discarded, by the `quality`/`balanced`/`budget` profile tables (FR-8.1), which are a strict generalization of §3's original 3-opus/10-sonnet split (preserved verbatim as the `quality` profile, FR-8.2). |
| FR-3 (rewrite PRD §1.4 NFR-4 to describe the tiered policy) | **Not touched by this feature — already fulfilled historically.** §3 FR-3.1 performed this rewrite as part of §3's own (superseded) implementation; that text is not revised again here. |
| FR-4 (README documents per-agent tier; override = hand-edit frontmatter, then `bash install.sh`) | **Partially discarded.** The documentation *goal* is fulfilled by a new README subsection (Affected Components, Modified Files). The override *procedure* — hand-edit an agent's `model:` field directly, then reinstall — is explicitly discarded: it is the exact mechanism that produced the `fable` drift Section 3 recorded, and this feature's whole purpose is to replace ad hoc frontmatter edits with a rewrite `install.sh` performs and a receipt CI can check (FR-7, FR-9, FR-10). |
| FR-5 (CONTRIBUTING template defaults new agents to `model: sonnet`) | **Neither fulfilled nor discarded — out of scope, and named as a gap.** This feature does not edit `CONTRIBUTING.md`; see 10.10 Risk 6. |
| FR-6 (QA test case 1.1.3 asserts an exact 3-opus/10-sonnet filename split) | **Discarded, and already obsolete before this feature.** That assertion was stale the moment `agents/plan-critic.md` shipped at `opus` (Section 9), before this feature existed. FR-10's CI validator supersedes what TC-1.1.3 was checking; the QA document's own retirement is a named follow-up (10.10 Risk 7), not performed here (task scope excludes writing QA documentation). |

### 10.2 User Story

As a developer using this pipeline unattended, I want a one-line fix to cost one response and a real feature to still get the full documentation-first pipeline — decided automatically, with the reason stated, and safe to get wrong in the cheap direction because the pipeline escalates itself rather than silently shipping under-documented — and I want the model tier every agent runs at to be a deliberate, install-time choice that CI can verify was actually applied, instead of a fact I have to trust because nobody hand-edited a frontmatter file since the last time I checked.

### 10.3 Functional Requirements

#### FR-1: Triage — Classification Signals and Statement of Reason

The classification step. Defined once, authoritatively and self-sufficiently, in `skills/develop-feature/SKILL.md`'s new Phase 0 (so `/develop-feature` works identically whether or not the memory layer is installed, mirroring the self-sufficiency the skill's existing Preflight: Memory Layer Check already establishes); restated in `src/claude.md` for the unprefixed-request path, which has no skill invocation to fall back on. Both copies MUST state identical signals, and both state, verbatim, that "a CI check greps both copies for parity" — a claim discovered mid-review to be false (no such check existed anywhere in `scripts/ci/` or CI), and closed within this same feature by FR-1.9's validator rather than left as an unmechanized, hand-maintained duplication.

1. **FR-1.1 (when triage runs):** Triage MUST run as the first step of `/develop-feature` (a new Phase 0, before Phase 1: Bootstrap) and, per `src/claude.md`, as the first step taken in response to any unprefixed natural-language feature or fix request — before any `Edit`/`Write` tool call related to the requested change, and before invoking any subagent for it.
2. **FR-1.2 (the estimated file set — required, stated output):** Before classifying, the orchestrating model MUST state, in its own response, the specific file(s) it expects the change to touch (the "estimated file set"). This is required output, not a mental step — FR-2's escalation checks compare what actually happens against it.
3. **FR-1.3 (mechanical `full`-forcing signals, checked first — any one forces `full` regardless of file count):** classify `full` immediately, skipping FR-1.4/FR-1.5, when the request (a) asks for a new API route/endpoint, a new user-facing page/screen/flow, or a new external service integration; (b) requires a database schema/migration change (new table, column, or index); (c) touches authentication, authorization, or payment/billing logic — by keyword match against the request text, or by the estimated file set (FR-1.2) overlapping a path FR-1.7 marks sensitive; (d) the estimated file set contains more than 3 files.
4. **FR-1.4 (`fast`-tier signal — ALL of the following, and none of FR-1.3):** (a) the estimated file set contains exactly 1 file; (b) the change is one of: a spelling/grammar fix in a comment, docstring, or user-facing copy string; a change to a single hardcoded literal (a constant, a config default, a version string, a URL, a timeout number) with no accompanying logic change; a comment-only edit; or a dependency-version bump requiring no source change. A request satisfying both is classified `fast`.
5. **FR-1.5 (`quick`-tier signal):** a request not forced to `full` by FR-1.3 and not satisfying FR-1.4 is classified `quick` when the estimated file set (FR-1.2) contains between 1 and 3 files and describes one bounded, already-understood behavior (a bug with a known root cause, a missing validation, a small new utility function, an adjustment to an existing function's or endpoint's behavior) with no new user-facing flow and no new architectural component.
6. **FR-1.6 (residual `full`):** any request not classified `fast` (FR-1.4) or `quick` (FR-1.5), or forced by FR-1.3, is classified `full` — including any request the model cannot confidently place in `fast` or `quick`. `full` is the tier of default safety, never a positive signal of its own.
7. **FR-1.7 (sensitive paths — union, never replace):** the fixed default list is ALWAYS active: any path containing `auth`, `payment`, `billing`, `secret`, or `migration` as a path segment (case-insensitive); any path under `.github/workflows/`; `install.sh`; `.claude/settings.json`; `docs/PRD.md`. A project's `.claude/rules/security.md` MAY additionally declare a `## Sensitive Paths` section listing further path globs (see Affected Components); triage treats a path as sensitive for FR-1.3(c) and FR-2.1/FR-2.2(b) when it matches EITHER the fixed default OR a declared entry. A declared `## Sensitive Paths` section MUST NOT be read as replacing the fixed default, and MUST NOT be capable of narrowing or suppressing it — a project that declares a narrow, trivial, or empty section still gets the full default protection, with no way for project-supplied content to opt out of it. This is a security-relevant decision, not a convenience default: `.claude/rules/security.md` is untrusted, project-supplied input feeding a classification decision, which is why 10.10 lists FR-1.7 as its own mandatory `security-auditor` pre-review item.
8. **FR-1.8 (stated reason, mandatory):** whichever tier is assigned — by FR-1.3 through FR-1.6, or by an FR-6 override — the response MUST state the tier and the specific signal that produced it (e.g., "tier: fast — single-file copy edit, no sensitive path" or "tier: full — FR-1.3(a), new API endpoint") before any `Edit`/`Write` call for the requested change. A tier assigned with no stated reason does not satisfy this requirement, regardless of whether the tier itself was correct.
9. **FR-1.9 (triage-parity CI check — closes the false-safety-net gap the intro above names):** a new zero-dependency Node validator, `scripts/ci/validate-triage-parity.js`, extracts the Steps 1-7 classification block (from the first line matching `**Step 1 —` to the first `**Tier branch —` line following it, treating fenced code blocks as opaque) from both `skills/develop-feature/SKILL.md` and `src/claude.md` and asserts the two blocks are byte-identical. On divergence, it splits both blocks into per-`**Step N —` chunks and reports which specific step differs, with a short context snippet at the first differing character, rather than merely reporting "files differ." Independently of the byte-comparison, it also re-asserts that both copies still enumerate all 9 of Step 6's fixed sensitive-path defaults (`auth`, `payment`, `billing`, `secret`, `migration`, `.github/workflows/`, `install.sh`, `.claude/settings.json`, `docs/PRD.md`) — a default silently dropped from BOTH copies in the same edit would otherwise pass a parity-only diff while still being a security regression, since that list is what forces `full` routing under FR-1.3(c).

#### FR-2: One-Way Escalation

1. **FR-2.1 (`fast` → `quick` trigger):** while operating under `fast` tier, if an `Edit`/`Write` call would target a file outside the FR-1.2 estimated file set, or a path FR-1.7 marks sensitive, that call MUST NOT be made under `fast`-tier discipline — escalate to `quick` per FR-2.3 first.
2. **FR-2.2 (`quick` → `full` triggers — two independent conditions):** while operating under `quick` tier, escalate to `full` per FR-2.4 — overriding whatever default behavior the triggering condition would otherwise have — when EITHER: (a) a Rule 4 condition (`src/rules/error-recovery.md` — architectural decisions, new dependencies, API contract changes, schema migrations) is encountered during FR-4's TDD cycle, in which case the run MUST NOT follow Rule 4's default "stop implementation, present to user" behavior; OR (b) an `Edit`/`Write` call would target a path FR-1.7 marks sensitive, or would bring the count of distinct files touched by this run's own `Edit`/`Write` calls above 3. Condition (b) exists because FR-1.3(c)/(d)'s mechanical signals are otherwise enforced at classification time only — a `quick`-tier change ("add a missing validation" is the canonical case) can sprawl mid-run into exactly the auth-path or file-count territory FR-1.3 would have forced to `full` had it been visible up front, and FR-1's one-time classification has no mechanism to catch that on its own. Condition (b) MUST be checked before the triggering `Edit`/`Write` call is made, exactly as FR-2.1 requires for its own trigger.
3. **FR-2.3 (fast→quick mechanics):** (a) state, verbatim, that escalation is occurring and name the second file or sensitive path; (b) leave any already-made edit(s) in place — never revert solely because of the escalation; (c) initialize `.claude/scratchpad.md` with `## Tier: quick` and a `## Feature:` name, recording the already-touched file(s) as already-completed context; (d) proceed under FR-4 for the remainder of the work, with the already-touched file(s) included in the single slice's `Files:`/`Changes:` fields as work already done; (e) clause (c)'s initialization, like every other `.claude/scratchpad.md` mutation this FR performs, MUST use **Edit, never a whole-file Write**, whenever the file already exists — which is the common case, since `.claude/scratchpad.md` is the pipeline's persistent, cross-feature memory (`src/rules/scratchpad.md`'s `## Completed` history) and is very likely already populated by the time a `fast`-tier run escalates; only the genuine first-ever-feature case, where no scratchpad file exists at all, legitimately uses `Write`, since that write targets a file that does not yet exist and is therefore outside `pre:write:shrink-guard`'s scope (Section 8 FR-3).
4. **FR-2.4 (quick→full mechanics — tier rewrite is its own mandatory step):** (a) state, verbatim, that escalation to `full` is occurring and name the specific FR-2.2 condition that fired (the Rule 4 condition, or the sensitive-path/file-count trigger); (b) leave any already-committed `quick`-tier slice commit(s) in place, never reverted; (c) **rewrite `.claude/scratchpad.md`'s `## Tier:` field from `quick` to `full` immediately, before any further gate or agent invocation.** This step is what makes clause (f)'s "indistinguishable at completion" claim true, not merely aspirational: without it, FR-4.7's Tier Check preamble would read the stale `quick` value and `/merge-ready` would silently run the reduced gate subset — skipping Gates 1/5/6/7/8 — on a feature this FR requires to get all 9; and `session:start:spine`'s state re-injection after a mid-run context compaction would re-surface the same stale `quick` value rather than the corrected one, since that hook injects whatever the scratchpad currently says. (c) makes the scratchpad the single, immediately-updated source of truth, so nothing downstream — including a post-escalation compaction — can observe the pre-escalation tier; (d) invoke `/bootstrap-feature` for the full, now-larger scope, supplying the already-completed work as context so `prd-writer`/`ba-analyst`/`architect`/`qa-planner` document it accurately rather than purely prospectively; (e) have `planner` mark any already-satisfied slice DONE with its existing commit hash in the resulting plan (the existing scratchpad DONE-with-commit-hash convention), never re-implemented; (f) proceed through the remaining slices and an unmodified, full 9-gate `/merge-ready` (FR-5) — indistinguishable at completion from a request classified `full` from the start, because by (c) `/merge-ready` reads `## Tier: full` exactly as it would have from the start; (g) clause (c)'s rewrite, and every other mutation of an already-existing `.claude/scratchpad.md` this FR performs, MUST use **Edit, never a whole-file Write** — `pre:write:shrink-guard` (Section 8 FR-3) fires on `Write` only and denies new content below `max(floor(old_lines × 0.4), 40)` lines, a threshold a small `quick`-tier scratchpad rewritten over a large pre-existing one would fail, whose sole escape (`SDLC_ALLOW_SHRINK=1`) is an environment variable unavailable mid-session — dead-ending exactly the escalation NFR-1(b) forbids from dead-ending; only a genuinely nonexistent scratchpad (the first-ever-feature case) legitimately uses `Write`, since that write targets a file that does not yet exist and is therefore outside the guard's scope.
5. **FR-2.5 (`full` is the escalation ceiling):** a Rule 4 condition encountered while already at `full` tier retains today's unmodified behavior (stop, present to the user, count against the retry budget) — there is no higher tier. FR-2.2's redirection applies only to a run currently at `quick`.
6. **FR-2.6 (no automatic downgrade — its own requirement):** once a tier is assigned — by FR-1 or by an FR-2.1/FR-2.2 escalation — it MUST NOT be automatically lowered for the remainder of that run, regardless of what later evaluation of actual scope suggests. A `quick`-tier run that turns out to touch only 1 file, or a `full`-tier run that turns out to need only one slice, completes at the tier it is already running under. The only way to run below what FR-1 would currently assign is an explicit, human-invoked override (FR-6) issued before the run starts — never a pipeline decision taken mid-run.
7. **FR-2.7 (named enforcement gap):** FR-2.1's trigger, and FR-2.2(b)'s equivalent mid-quick-run trigger, are both enforced by the orchestrating model's own compliance with FR-1.8/FR-2.1/FR-2.2 — no hook checks `Edit`/`Write` call counts or paths against the declared tier in this feature. See 10.10 Risk 1.
8. **FR-2.8 (`## Tier:` is always affirmatively owned, never left to inherit a stale value):** `skills/bootstrap-feature/SKILL.md` Step 7 (Initialize Scratchpad) MUST write an explicit `## Tier:` field every time it initializes or reinitializes a scratchpad — `full`, for both a fresh, non-escalated `/bootstrap-feature` run and for the run FR-2.4(d) invokes (where FR-2.4(c) has already set the field to `full`, so Step 7's write is a confirming no-op, not a second, independent source of truth). This is the general-case fix behind Critical Finding 1: a scratchpad field written by one path and read by another MUST be affirmatively owned at every initialization point, never left to whatever value happened to be there before.

#### FR-3: Fast Tier Execution

1. **FR-3.1:** under `fast` tier, the orchestrating model makes the `Edit`/`Write` call(s) directly — no `Agent`/`Task` call, no `docs/PRD.md`/`docs/use-cases/*`/`docs/qa/*` file created or modified for this change, no plan written to `.claude/scratchpad.md`'s `## Plan` section. For a target file that already exists, the model MUST `Read` it in this session before the `Edit` call — satisfying `pre:edit:read-guard`'s requirement (Section 8 FR-4) rather than bouncing off it on the run's first edit; a `Write` creating a brand-new file requires no prior `Read` (Section 8 FR-4.4).
2. **FR-3.2 (verification):** after editing, the model runs the project's declared build/typecheck command directly via its own `Bash` call, reusing `stop:typecheck-format`'s (Section 7) existing "read the command from the project's CLAUDE.md; no-op visibly when none is declared" contract rather than inventing a second one.
3. **FR-3.3 (commit):** follows `src/rules/git.md` unchanged — feature branch, conventional commit, no AI attribution — identical to every other tier. `pre:bash:git-guard` (Section 8), where installed, enforces this mechanically with no tier-awareness needed.
4. **FR-3.4 (changelog — mandatory, sole owner):** after a successful commit, the model MUST write one `CHANGELOG.md` entry directly, following the identical standalone-fix procedure `skills/implement-slice/SKILL.md` Step 6 already defines (real `date -u` timestamp, idempotency guard, ≤500-character Details). No `/merge-ready` run occurs for `fast` tier, so this write is never suppressed by a `no-changelog` flag and is owned by nothing downstream — skipping it is not an option.
5. **FR-3.5:** a `fast`-tier run that does not escalate does not write to `.claude/scratchpad.md` at all — there is no multi-step state to persist.

#### FR-4: Quick Tier Execution

1. **FR-4.1 (the one subagent):** under `quick` tier, the model invokes `planner` exactly once, under a new narrow invocation mode added to `agents/planner.md` alongside its existing Replan Contract and Conflict Recovery Contract — the **Quick-Tier Contract**. Given a plain feature/fix description (no PRD section, use-cases file, QA file, or architecture review as input — none exist for a `quick`-tier change), `planner` returns exactly one slice in the standard `Files:`/`Changes:`/`Verify:`/`Done when:` format, skipping its default Process step 1 documentation-existence read entirely for this mode. The returned slice MUST NOT carry a `**Tracer:** yes` marker — quick tier's single slice is exempt from Section 9 FR-8's tracer-first requirement by design (FR-4.5 below states the resulting notice-wording requirement for `/implement-slice`, so the marker's absence is never mislabeled as a legacy plan).
2. **FR-4.2 (the one plan file):** `planner` has no `Write`/`Edit` tool and returns the slice; the orchestrator writes it into `.claude/scratchpad.md`'s `## Plan` section — the same location and format `/bootstrap-feature` Step 7 already uses — as a single, un-waved slice, together with `## Tier: quick` and a `## Feature:` name. No second, separate plan-file format is introduced.
3. **FR-4.3 (TDD, and the mandatory `no-changelog` token — the sole path to a single changelog entry):** the model runs `/implement-slice` against this one slice, **passing the literal `no-changelog` token** — exactly as `/develop-feature` Phase 2 already does for full-tier slices — otherwise unmodified except for FR-4.4's bypass: `test-writer` writes tests first, the model implements, `build-runner` verifies, the slice commits per `src/rules/git.md`. The token is mandatory, not optional: without it, both of `/implement-slice` Step 6's skip conditions are false (no wave context, and no `no-changelog` token), so Step 6 writes a standalone changelog entry under its own name while FR-4.7's Finalization step separately writes a second entry under the feature's own name for the same unit of work — two entries where Section 5's exactly-once ownership rule requires one, and the idempotency guard (keyed on entry name) does not catch it because the two entries' names differ.
4. **FR-4.4 (`skills/implement-slice/SKILL.md` Pre-flight Check 4 and `test-writer` delegation, both tier-aware):** Pre-flight Check 4 (confirm `docs/qa/*`/`docs/use-cases/*` exist) MUST be skipped entirely when `.claude/scratchpad.md`'s `## Tier:` reads `quick` — these documents are never required for a `quick`-tier change, by design. Additionally, Step 2's existing delegation to `test-writer` — whose Process step 1 unconditionally reads `docs/qa/<feature>_test_cases.md` — MUST, for a `quick`-tier run, state verbatim in the delegation prompt that no QA test-case file exists for this change by design and MUST NOT be treated as a missing input; `test-writer` derives its tests from the slice's own `Verify:`/`Done when:` fields instead. Both checks run unmodified for every run where `## Tier:` reads `full` or is absent (a legacy, pre-F4 scratchpad).
5. **FR-4.5 (tier-aware tracer-gate notice):** `/implement-slice`'s existing tracer-gate check, encountering FR-4.1's marker-free single slice under `## Tier: quick`, MUST NOT print the existing "tracer gate inactive — no `**Tracer:** yes` marker found; treating as pre-F3 plan." notice — that wording misdescribes a post-F4 `quick`-tier plan as a legacy, pre-F3 one. It MUST instead print: "tracer gate inactive — tier: quick, single-slice plan is exempt from the tracer requirement by design." The existing pre-F3 wording is retained, unchanged, for every plan where `## Tier:` is absent or reads `full`.
6. **FR-4.6 (reduced quality gates, under a fail-closed default):** the model runs `/merge-ready` after the slice commits, under the tier-aware gate subset FR-4.7 defines rather than the full 9-gate sequence — and FR-4.7's Tier Check preamble is fail-closed: the reduced subset is granted only to the literal value `quick`, never inferred for any other or malformed value.
7. **FR-4.7 (`skills/merge-ready/SKILL.md` Tier Check preamble, fail-closed, including tier-conditional Gate 2/3 delegation):** `/merge-ready` gains an unnumbered preamble step, before Gate 0 — mirroring the existing unnumbered "Finalization: Changelog Entry" postamble's precedent for adding a step without renumbering the gates — that reads `.claude/scratchpad.md`'s `## Tier:` field under a **fail-closed rule: any value other than the literal string `quick` runs all 9 gates unmodified.** `full`, the field absent, `fast`, a typo, or merge-conflict garbage left in the field are all treated identically — this is the rule itself, not merely a fallback for the two cases spelled out next. `full`, or the field absent, runs all 9 gates unmodified (absent-means-`full` is the backward-compatibility instance of the fail-closed rule, not a separate case). `quick` runs Gate 0 (Git Hygiene), Gate 2 (Code Review), Gate 3 (Security Audit), and Gate 4 (Build Verification), and reports Gate 1, Gate 5, Gate 6, Gate 7, and Gate 8 as `SKIPPED (tier: quick)` in the output table rather than running or silently omitting them. **Gate 2's and Gate 3's delegation prompts to `code-reviewer`/`security-auditor`, for a `quick`-tier run, MUST state verbatim that no `docs/PRD.md` section, `docs/use-cases/*`, or `docs/qa/*` file exists for this change by design and MUST NOT be reported as a finding** — this is what stops `agents/code-reviewer.md`'s existing "Test cases documented in `docs/qa/`" checklist item (and any analogous PRD/use-case item either reviewer would otherwise apply) from being flagged, and stops the Auto-Fix Protocol from exhausting its 3 attempts trying to fix a document that was never supposed to exist, reporting `NOT MERGE READY` on a correctly-scoped `quick`-tier change. This context supersedes the checklist item for the duration of a `quick`-tier delegation only — neither agent's own file is edited by this feature. The Finalization step's trigger condition ("runs ONLY after all gates report PASS") is read, for a `quick`-tier run, as "all gates that were not `SKIPPED` report PASS" — a `SKIPPED` gate neither blocks finalization nor counts as a FAIL. **Residual risk, recorded rather than resolved:** `.claude/scratchpad.md` is a tracked file, so a hostile repository could pre-commit the literal `## Tier: quick` to attempt downgrading a *standalone* `/merge-ready` run before `/bootstrap-feature` ever gets a chance to re-own and reset the field itself (FR-2.8). This is mitigated, not eliminated: skipped gates always render as explicit `SKIPPED (tier: quick)` rows in the output table (never a silently blank cell), and Gate 2 (Code Review) and Gate 3 (Security Audit) still run against the diff regardless of tier.
8. **FR-4.8 (why Gates 2/3/4 are kept):** Gate 2 and Gate 3 review the diff itself and do not depend on any PRD/use-case/QA artifact; Gate 4 is deterministic. Skipping them would make `quick` a synonym for "unreviewed" — keeping them is what stops that.

#### FR-5: Full Tier

1. **FR-5.1:** a request classified `full` (FR-1.6 or FR-2.4's escalation) follows `skills/develop-feature/SKILL.md`'s existing Phase 1 through Phase 3 pipeline exactly as it exists today, with no change introduced by this feature beyond the new Phase 0 (FR-1) preceding it and the Tier Check preamble (FR-4.7) at `/merge-ready`, which for `full` tier runs all 9 gates unmodified.

#### FR-6: Override Skills — `/sdlc-fast`, `/sdlc-quick`

1. **FR-6.1:** two new skill files, `skills/sdlc-fast/SKILL.md` and `skills/sdlc-quick/SKILL.md`, each with `argument-hint: "<description>"` and `arguments: [feature]`. `sdlc-fast`'s `allowed-tools` is `Read, Glob, Grep, Edit, Write, Bash, Agent` — the `Agent` tool IS granted, deliberately: FR-2.1's mandated escalation from `fast` to `quick` requires invoking `planner`, `test-writer`, and `build-runner` (all `Agent` calls), so withholding `Agent` would make `sdlc-fast` unable to perform its own required escalation, dead-ending an unattended run in violation of NFR-1(b) the moment a `fast`-tier request under this override turned out to need a second file. The "no subagent" constraint for the ordinary, non-escalated `fast` path (FR-3.1) is therefore enforced by instruction, not by tool grant — consistent with FR-2.7's accepted model-discipline posture for the rest of this feature's tier-boundary enforcement, not a structural guarantee. `sdlc-quick`'s `allowed-tools` matches `develop-feature`'s, since it drives `planner`/`implement-slice`/`merge-ready`.
2. **FR-6.2:** `/sdlc-fast <description>` and `/sdlc-quick <description>` bypass FR-1's classification entirely and run FR-3 or FR-4 directly against the supplied description — the human asserts the tier; the pipeline does not compute it. FR-2's escalation rules still apply once running: an `/sdlc-fast` invocation that turns out to touch a second file still escalates to `quick`; an `/sdlc-quick` invocation that hits Rule 4 still escalates to `full`. The override skips classification, never the safety rails.
3. **FR-6.3 (literal-token flag rule, applied to tier selection itself):** the override activates ONLY via the literal invocation of `/sdlc-fast` or `/sdlc-quick` as an actually-invoked slash command — the same literal-token discipline already governing `no-changelog` (`skills/implement-slice/SKILL.md`) and every other documented flag in this harness. A request whose prose merely contains a word like "quick," "fast," "small," or "trivial" MUST still be classified by FR-1 unmodified; the pipeline MUST NOT infer the override from vocabulary. `src/claude.md`'s unprefixed-request path has no skill invocation to check a literal token against, so this override is unavailable there by construction — the two skills are the sole entry point.
4. **FR-6.4 (skill count):** these two skills bring the total from 5 to 7, against the v4.0 budget of ≤10 skills.

#### FR-7: Model Routing — `install.sh --profile` Mechanism

1. **FR-7.1:** `install.sh` gains a `--profile <quality|balanced|budget|inherit>` flag: a distinct mode, mutually exclusive with `--uninstall`, `--restore`, `--init-project`, and `--trust-project` (refuse with a named error, mirroring the existing mutual-exclusivity checks in `main()`), and REQUIRING `--local`. `install.sh --profile <name>` without `--local` MUST refuse with an error naming the requirement: the rewrite targets `agents/*.md` inside the plugin-source checkout `/plugin marketplace add <path>` will later point at; a non-`--local` run's source directory is a `mktemp -d` clone `cleanup_source_dir` deletes before the process exits, so a rewrite there would be silently discarded.
2. **FR-7.2 (the rewrite — awk-bounded, atomic, two-phase preflight-then-commit, never bare `sed`/`sed -i`):** for each of the 14 files under `$SCRIPT_DIR/agents/*.md`, rewrite the `model:` line WITHIN the file's YAML frontmatter (between its first and second `---` fence line, never elsewhere in the file) to the value FR-8.1's table assigns that filename's role under the chosen profile. The mechanism MUST satisfy all of the following, mirroring the existing preflight-then-copy discipline `install_user_config()` already uses for the manifest-driven memory-layer install ("Preflight EVERY entry — source present and destination safe — before copying any of them... this way a rejected entry leaves nothing installed at all"):
   - **(a) Bounded substitution, not a bare pattern over the whole file:** use `awk`, tracking a frontmatter-fence counter, to replace only a `model: ` line encountered between the first and second `---` line. A `sed "s/^model: .*/.../"` applied to the whole file would also match a `model: ` string appearing anywhere in the agent's prompt body by coincidence — the frontmatter bound MUST be structural, not assumed from the convention that agent bodies do not currently contain such a line.
   - **(b) Same-directory temp file, never bare `mktemp`:** stage each rewritten file with `tmp="$(mktemp "agents/.${file}.XXXXXX")"` — inside the same directory as the target, exactly as `write_receipt()` already does for `.sdlc-receipt` — so the later `mv` is a same-filesystem rename and therefore atomic. A bare `mktemp` (defaulting to `$TMPDIR`) can stage on a different filesystem, silently degrading the `mv` into a non-atomic copy.
   - **(c) Per-file post-substitution assertion — zero substitutions is a failure, not a silent no-op success:** a `sed`/`awk` pipeline piped into `mv` exits `0` on zero matches, so a file missing its `model:` line, or one whose frontmatter fence is malformed, would "succeed" while changing nothing — and FR-9's receipt would then claim a profile that was not actually applied to that file. Each per-file rewrite MUST assert exactly one substitution occurred and fail loudly otherwise, illustrated here:
     ```
     tmp="$(mktemp "agents/.${file}.XXXXXX")"
     awk -v target="model: ${target}" '
       BEGIN { fences = 0; matched = 0 }
       {
         if ($0 == "---") { fences++; print; next }
         if (fences == 1 && $0 ~ /^model: /) { print target; matched++; next }
         print
       }
       END { if (matched != 1) { print "ERROR: expected exactly one model: line in frontmatter, found " matched > "/dev/stderr"; exit 1 } }
     ' "agents/${file}" > "$tmp" || { rm -f "$tmp"; exit 1; }
     ```
   - **(d) Preflight over all 14 before any commit — a partial failure leaves zero files rewritten, not N of 14:** perform (a) through (c) for all 14 files first, writing each result to its own validated temp file, WITHOUT `mv`-ing any of them into place. Only after all 14 pass does a second pass `mv` each temp file over its target. If any of the 14 fails preflight, abort immediately, remove every temp file already created, and exit non-zero — the tree MUST show zero modified source files in that case, never a state where some of the 14 reflect the new profile and others do not.
   - **(e) Both `mktemp` calls guarded, plus a whole-lifecycle `EXIT` trap (security-audit hardening):** the per-file temp-file `mktemp` in (b) and the profile-receipt's own temp-file `mktemp` (FR-9.2) MUST each be guarded — `tmp="$(mktemp ...)" || { ...; exit 1; }` — so a `mktemp` failure (a read-only or full filesystem) aborts the run immediately rather than proceeding with an unset `tmp` variable under `set -e`. In addition, `trap cleanup_profile_tempfiles EXIT` MUST be installed for the full duration of the preflight-then-commit lifecycle (cleared only once Phase 2 has fully committed all 14 files), so an unhandled exit — a `SIGINT`, or `set -e` firing on any other failure inside the lifecycle — still removes every temp file already staged under `agents/`, not only the paths that call the cleanup function explicitly.
3. **FR-7.3 (`inherit` is a literal value, never field deletion):** under `--profile inherit`, every agent's `model:` line is rewritten to the literal string `model: inherit` via the identical FR-7.2 mechanism — the field is never deleted. `scripts/ci/validate-agents.js`'s `REQUIRED_FIELDS` already requires `model` present and non-empty, and `inherit` is already a member of its `VALID_MODEL_ALIASES` set (Section 6 FR-5.2); deleting the field would fail that existing, unrelated validator and would be indistinguishable from the accidental-omission failure mode this feature exists to close.
4. **FR-7.4 (no other field touched):** the rewrite touches only the `model:` line. `name`, `description`, `tools`, `effort:` (FR-11), and every line of the prompt body MUST be byte-identical before and after — mirroring Section 3's original FR-1.11 precedent for the same class of constraint.
5. **FR-7.5 (`--dry-run` support):** `install.sh --local --profile <name> --dry-run` prints, per file, the current `model:` value and the value the chosen profile would write, modifying nothing — mirroring the existing `--dry-run` behavior for `--uninstall`. The printed *current* value is untrusted content read from the file being previewed; it MUST be stripped of control characters (`tr -d '[:cntrl:]'`) before being printed, mirroring `say_untrusted()`'s existing discipline for untrusted strings elsewhere in `install.sh` (manifest entries, receipt lines) — a `model:` line crafted to contain raw escape bytes MUST NOT be able to rewrite the terminal or forge output during a preview.
6. **FR-7.6 (mandatory spike — whether a running session picks up a profile rewrite, mirroring FR-13.4's discipline):** before this mechanism ships, the implementation MUST determine, and record in `install.sh`'s own header comment plus a README note, whether a Claude Code session already running against an installed plugin re-reads `agents/*.md` frontmatter live or snapshots agent definitions at plugin load/session start. If it snapshots, `--profile`'s rewrite of the plugin-source checkout does not take effect in an already-running session even though the receipt (FR-9) and CI (FR-10) both now validate the rewritten checkout — precisely the class of drift this feature exists to close, just relocated from "hand-edited frontmatter" to "stale running session." The README note MUST state plainly whether a new session, a `/plugin` reinstall, or neither is required after `install.sh --profile` for the new `model:` values to take effect.

#### FR-8: Model Profile Tables

1. **FR-8.1 (the table, exact):**

   | Role | `quality` | `balanced` | `budget` | `inherit` |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | architect | opus | opus | sonnet | inherit |
   | plan-critic | opus | sonnet | sonnet | inherit |
   | planner | opus | opus | sonnet | inherit |
   | security-auditor | opus | opus | opus | inherit |
   | ba-analyst | sonnet | sonnet | sonnet | inherit |
   | build-runner | sonnet | haiku | haiku | inherit |
   | code-reviewer | sonnet | sonnet | sonnet | inherit |
   | doc-updater | sonnet | haiku | haiku | inherit |
   | e2e-runner | sonnet | sonnet | sonnet | inherit |
   | prd-writer | sonnet | haiku | haiku | inherit |
   | qa-planner | sonnet | sonnet | sonnet | inherit |
   | refactor-cleaner | sonnet | sonnet | haiku | inherit |
   | test-writer | sonnet | sonnet | haiku | inherit |
   | verifier | sonnet | sonnet | sonnet | inherit |

2. **FR-8.2 (`quality` equals the shipped baseline):** the `quality` column's 14 values MUST equal, role for role, the `model:` values already committed in `agents/*.md` (4 `opus` — `architect`, `plan-critic`, `planner`, `security-auditor`; 10 `sonnet` — the rest; verified directly against all 14 files during this section's drafting). This is what lets FR-10's drift check treat "no `.sdlc-model-profile` receipt present" and "receipt present, reading `quality`" as the identical, validated state (FR-9.3).
3. **FR-8.3 (rationale for the `balanced`/`budget` divergence from Section 3's original three-opus set):** Section 3 kept `architect`, `planner`, and `security-auditor` on `opus` because "a wrong call here is multiplied across every slice" and "mistakes aren't catchable by automated verification." Two of those three premises no longer hold as originally stated, now that Section 9 has shipped: `plan-critic`'s adversarial review (Section 9 FR-5) catches a bad `architect`/`planner` verdict before implementation begins, and Gate 6's `--gaps` replan loop (Section 9 FR-10) catches a resulting integration gap before merge — neither backstop existed when Section 3 was drafted. This is the "F4 model-tier review" Section 9 FR-5.1 explicitly deferred `plan-critic`'s own tier to. `security-auditor`'s premise is unchanged — a missed vulnerability still ships silently with no downstream backstop of the same kind — which is why it alone stays `opus` under every profile including `budget`. `plan-critic` drops to `sonnet` under `balanced`/`budget` because its check is a FORCE-stance pass against enumerable, listed criteria (Section 9 FR-5.2), more mechanical than the open-ended judgment `architect`/`planner` exercise, and it already carries its own structural backstop (the 3-loop-then-escalate mechanism, Section 9 FR-5.5).

#### FR-9: Install Receipt

1. **FR-9.1:** a new file `.sdlc-model-profile`, at the repository root (sibling to `agents/`, inside the plugin-source checkout — never under `~/.claude`, which this flag never touches), contains exactly one line: the applied profile name.
2. **FR-9.2 (atomic write):** written only after all 14 rewrites (FR-7.2) succeed, via the same temp-file-then-`mv` pattern `write_receipt()` already uses for `.sdlc-receipt` — an interrupted or partially-failed rewrite MUST NOT leave a receipt claiming a profile that was not fully applied.
3. **FR-9.3 (absence means `quality`):** when `.sdlc-model-profile` is absent, FR-10's drift check treats the tree as `quality` — valid only because of FR-8.2's equality guarantee.
4. **FR-9.4 (never committed — a named rule, not merely a `.gitignore` convention):** `.sdlc-model-profile` MUST NEVER be committed to version control. FR-7's `.gitignore` entry (Slice 1) is the passive half of this rule; FR-10.6's `--assert-baseline` flag is the active half CI actually enforces — the receipt is a local, install-time artifact describing what one contributor's checkout currently has applied, never a fact about the repository itself. A committed receipt is not merely redundant, it is actively dangerous: FR-10.2's per-file check reads whichever profile the receipt names, so a contributor who commits both a rewritten `agents/` tree and its matching receipt produces a tree FR-10.2 alone would validate as internally consistent — the receipt would silently authorize exactly the class of committed, undocumented model-tier change this feature exists to make visible.

#### FR-10: CI Drift Check

1. **FR-10.1:** a new zero-dependency Node validator `scripts/ci/validate-model-profile.js`, following the existing `core.run(...)`/`Validator`/`--root`/`--expect-failure` pattern every validator in `scripts/ci/` already uses (verified present at HEAD in `scripts/ci/lib/validate-core.js`), plus a new shared table module `scripts/ci/lib/model-profiles.js` exporting FR-8.1's table.
2. **FR-10.2 (repo/profile agreement — a distinct check from the existing alias check):** for each `agents/*.md` file under `--root`, read `.sdlc-model-profile` (or default to `quality` per FR-9.3) and assert the file's `model:` value equals the table's entry for `<resolved profile>:<role>`. Any other value — including a recognized-but-unauthorized alias like `fable`, which `scripts/ci/validate-agents.js`'s existing `VALID_MODEL_ALIASES` check accepts without checking whether it is the right value for that role and profile — is reported by file, naming the value found and the value expected. This is deliberately distinct from `validate-agents.js`'s check: that one is a liberal floor (is this a recognized alias at all); this one is the strict, profile-specific check. It does not modify `validate-agents.js`'s alias set.
3. **FR-10.3 (install.sh/CI table agreement — bidirectional):** `install.sh`'s rewrite logic MUST encode FR-8.1's table as shell `case` arms in the exact shape `<profile>:<role>) echo <model> ;;` (one arm per profile/role pair, or a single `inherit:*) echo inherit ;;` wildcard standing in for all 14 `inherit` rows). `scripts/ci/validate-model-profile.js` MUST additionally parse `install.sh`'s text for this shape — a text scan; it never executes `install.sh` — and assert the extracted `(profile, role, model)` triples exactly match `scripts/ci/lib/model-profiles.js`'s table, treating a matched `inherit:*)` wildcard as satisfying all 14 `inherit` rows. This comparison MUST be bidirectional, closing a completeness gap a parse-then-compare-only design leaves open: it is not enough for every triple the parser DID extract to match the JS table (Direction 1) — a case arm deleted outright, or rewritten into a shape `CASE_ARM_RE` no longer matches (e.g. a quoted `echo` value), produces no parse result at all and would be silently skipped by a check that only iterates what it successfully parsed. The validator MUST therefore also (Direction 2) assert the full expected `<profile>:<role>` matrix — every non-`inherit` profile × every known role, since `inherit` rows are covered by the single wildcard arm — was actually extracted, naming any missing pair by its `profile:role` key; and MUST (Direction 3) report, by name, any line inside `model_for_role()`'s body that is neither a recognized case arm, the `inherit:*)` wildcard, nor known function scaffolding (the signature, local-var declaration, `case`/`esac` bracketing, catch-all arm, blank lines, comments) as an unparseable line — since `bash` would still execute such a line even though this validator's per-arm check would otherwise miss it. A hand-edit to either table that diverges from the other — including one that deletes an arm outright or rewrites it into a non-matching shape — fails this check by name.
4. **FR-10.4 (anti-vacuity and seeded-bad fixtures):** fails on an empty/absent `agents/` tree (`v.requireMinimum`, mirroring every existing validator). `tests/fixtures/ci/model-profile/*` MUST include at minimum: (a) `.sdlc-model-profile` reading `budget` with one file's `model:` left at its `quality` value (`stale-file`); (b) no `.sdlc-model-profile` (implicit `quality`) with one file reading `model: fable` (`no-receipt-fable`); (c) a fixture `install.sh` copy whose `case` table disagrees with `scripts/ci/lib/model-profiles.js` (`install-table-mismatch`); (d) a malformed `.sdlc-model-profile` value outside `{quality, balanced, budget, inherit}`, its own named failure per UC-14-EC1, never silently defaulted (`bad-receipt`); (e) an all-correct positive fixture for the `inherit` profile whose `install.sh` copy uses only the single `inherit:*)` wildcard arm, proving the wildcard-match logic rather than requiring 14 individual `inherit` arms (`inherit-wildcard`); (f) FR-10.6's `committed-receipt` boundary fixture — internally consistent (receipt and tree agree), so it passes plainly and fails only under `--assert-baseline`, proving that flag is load-bearing rather than decorative.
5. **FR-10.5 (CI wiring):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `validate-assets` job gains `node scripts/ci/validate-model-profile.js --assert-baseline` against the real tree, plus one `--expect-failure` step per FR-10.4 fixture, mirroring the existing Falsify/Anti-vacuity step pattern (Section 9 FR-11.5's precedent for `validate-verification-upgrade.js`).
6. **FR-10.6 (`--assert-baseline` — the never-commit-the-receipt guard, FR-9.4):** a new flag on `scripts/ci/validate-model-profile.js`. When passed, ANY `.sdlc-model-profile` present at `--root` is itself a failure, reported by name, independent of and in addition to FR-10.2's ordinary per-file comparison (which still runs against whatever profile the receipt — malformed or not — would otherwise resolve to). Without this flag, `scripts/ci/validate-model-profile.js` cannot distinguish a legitimate local install-time receipt from one a contributor mistakenly committed alongside a matching rewritten `agents/` tree; FR-10.4(f)'s `committed-receipt` fixture is the proof that the flag, not the ordinary check, is what catches that case. CI's real-tree run (FR-10.5) always passes this flag, since `.sdlc-model-profile` must never exist there (FR-9.4).

#### FR-11: `effort:` Frontmatter Field

1. **FR-11.1:** every file under `agents/*.md` (all 14) gains a new required, non-empty frontmatter field `effort:`, one of `low`, `medium`, `high`.
2. **FR-11.2 (assignment, exact):**

   | effort | Roles |
   |---|---|
   | low | build-runner, doc-updater, prd-writer |
   | medium | ba-analyst, code-reviewer, e2e-runner, qa-planner, refactor-cleaner, test-writer |
   | high | architect, planner, security-auditor, plan-critic, verifier |

3. **FR-11.3 (fixed, not profile-dependent):** `effort:` is NEVER rewritten by `install.sh --profile` — FR-7.4's "no other field touched" applies to it by name. `effort:` describes how much a role benefits from deeper reasoning within whatever model tier is currently assigned, a property of the role, not of the cost/quality tradeoff a profile encodes. The two axes are deliberately orthogonal: `verifier` is `effort: high` (careful, multi-level static analysis) but ships at `sonnet`, never `opus`, under every profile in FR-8.1 — a role can need careful reasoning without needing the largest model.
4. **FR-11.4 (`scripts/ci/validate-agents.js` modification):** `REQUIRED_FIELDS` gains `effort`; a new `VALID_EFFORT_LEVELS = new Set(['low', 'medium', 'high'])` check, mirroring the existing `VALID_MODEL_ALIASES` check's structure, rejects any other value.

#### FR-12: Digest Index for `planner`

1. **FR-12.1:** new file `docs/digest-index.md`. One row per `full`-tier feature whose documentation has been finalized through `/merge-ready` Gate 7: `| Section | Title | Summary (≤300 characters) | Docs |`, where `Docs` lists that feature's PRD section anchor, `docs/use-cases/<slug>_use_cases.md`, and `docs/qa/<slug>_test_cases.md`.
2. **FR-12.2 (who writes it, and when — an existing pipeline point):** `/merge-ready` Gate 7's existing delegation to `doc-updater` gains one more duty, for `full`-tier runs only (Gate 7 is `SKIPPED` for `quick` tier per FR-4.7 and never reached for `fast` tier, so neither ever produces a digest row): append, or — if a row for that section number already exists — refresh it in place, using the same idempotency discipline `src/rules/changelog.md`'s guard already establishes, keyed here on section number instead of entry name.
3. **FR-12.3 (`agents/planner.md` Process step 1, rewritten to eliminate the unbounded whole-file read):** today's Process step 1 instructs "Read `docs/PRD.md`" unqualified — a whole-file read with no section scoping, verified directly against the shipped file. It MUST be rewritten to read only the current feature's own PRD section (identified by the section number/title supplied in the delegation prompt, FR-12.5), its own `docs/use-cases/<feature>_use_cases.md`, architecture review output, and `docs/qa/<feature>_test_cases.md` — all in full, exactly as much as today, scoped to the current feature rather than the whole file.
4. **FR-12.4 (bounded prior-feature context):** immediately after FR-12.3's step, `planner` MUST read `docs/digest-index.md` in full (if present) and select between 2 and 4 rows most relevant to the feature being planned, by keyword/topic overlap with the current request, reading only those rows' referenced documents (their PRD section, not the whole `docs/PRD.md`, plus their `docs/use-cases/*`) in full. When fewer than 2 relevant rows exist — including an early-stage project where the index is empty or absent — `planner` reads however many are actually relevant (0 or 1) and proceeds without inventing relevance to reach 2. This bound applies only to PRIOR features' documents; FR-12.3's read of the current feature's own documents is unconditional.
5. **FR-12.5 (`skills/bootstrap-feature/SKILL.md` Step 5 delegation prompt):** since `planner` no longer discovers the current feature's section number by reading the whole PRD file, Step 5's delegation to `planner` MUST state the current feature's PRD section number and title explicitly.
6. **FR-12.6 (quick tier — unaffected):** FR-4's Quick-Tier Contract already skips the documentation-existence read entirely; it does not consult `docs/digest-index.md` either — a `quick`-tier change has no PRD section of its own to place in the index (FR-12.2) and gains nothing from context bounded for full-tier planning depth.

#### FR-13: Statusline

1. **FR-13.1:** new template file `templates/statusline.js`; `install.sh --init-project` copies it to the scaffolded project's `.claude/statusline.js` — a `cp`, never an execution, identical in kind to how `templates/settings.json`/`templates/CLAUDE.md` are already copied. This does not violate the no-node/no-jq constraint on `install.sh` (the existing CI step "Assert install.sh invokes neither node nor jq"): that check governs commands `install.sh` itself runs, not the content of a file it copies for Claude Code to invoke later — the identical distinction already holds for every `node "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/lib/run-hook.js"` string inside `hooks/hooks.json`, which `install.sh` never touches.
2. **FR-13.2:** `templates/settings.json` gains a `statusLine` field: `{"type": "command", "command": "node .claude/statusline.js"}`.
3. **FR-13.3 (rendered fields, exact order):** `<feature> | wave <W> slice <N>/<M> | gates <G>/9 | $<cost> | <usable-context bar>`, where:
   - `<feature>` is the scratchpad's `## Feature:` value, or `no active feature` when absent or reading `none active`;
   - `wave <W> slice <N>/<M>` is read from the scratchpad's `## Plan`: `N` is the 1-based position, within its own wave, of the currently IN PROGRESS (or next pending) slice; `M` is that wave's total slice count. Omitted entirely when no plan is present in the scratchpad — which includes the ordinary, non-escalated `fast`-tier case, since FR-3.5 means such a run never writes a scratchpad at all — or when every slice in the plan is DONE;
   - `gates <G>/9` is read from a `Gates: N/9` line `/merge-ready` writes to the scratchpad after each gate reaches a terminal PASS/FAIL/SKIPPED state — a new line, mirroring the exact precedent Section 9 FR-10.5 established for `Gate 6 attempts: N/3` (merge-ready-owned transient state, not a change to `src/rules/scratchpad.md`'s format definition). Omitted when no such line is present;
   - `$<cost>` is read directly from the statusline's own stdin JSON — the cost field Claude Code's native statusline invocation already supplies;
   - the usable-context bar is `(max_tokens − autocompact_reserve − used_tokens) / (max_tokens − autocompact_reserve)`, floored at 0%, using whichever field names FR-13.4's spike confirms.
4. **FR-13.4 (mandatory spike, mirroring Section 8 FR-6.1's precedent):** before finalizing FR-13.3's extraction logic, the implementation MUST invoke a real Claude Code session with the statusline configured, capture the actual stdin JSON, and record the exact field names for cost and context-window usage — including whether an autocompact-reserve value is exposed directly or must be approximated by a documented constant — in the handler's own header comment, the same "record the finding before writing the logic" discipline Section 8 established.
5. **FR-13.5 (fail-open-by-analogy — this feature's own contract):** `templates/statusline.js` MUST wrap its entire body in a top-level error boundary. On any error — missing or unparseable scratchpad, missing merge-ready progress line, a stdin payload missing an expected field — it MUST still print a minimal, non-empty status line containing at least the cost and usable-context segments it can still derive from its own stdin, rather than throwing, printing nothing, or crashing the renderer. This is stated as this feature's own requirement, not inherited from Section 7's hook fail-open contract (NFR-4: a statusline is not a hook).
6. **FR-13.6 (no reverse dependency — Risk 8's degrade-visibly framing):** no capability in this feature, or planned by the roadmap, reads the statusline's own output back into the pipeline. "Degrade visibly when the statusline is not running" therefore describes the statusline's own absence, which is Claude Code's native behavior when `statusLine` is unconfigured (no status line renders at all) — this feature adds no pipeline-side check for that absence, since nothing yet depends on it. Recorded so a future feature that DOES want statusline-sourced data builds its own visible-degradation check.
7. **FR-13.7 (hook budget):** this capability consumes zero of the ≤12 hook-budget slots — it registers no `hooks/hooks.json` entry and is not dispatched by `run-hook.js`.
8. **FR-13.8 (zero dependencies; visible failure without a Node runtime):** `templates/statusline.js` has zero npm runtime dependencies, consistent with every other Node artifact this harness ships (Section 6/7 NFR-1 lineage) — no `npm install` step is required. On a machine with no Node runtime, Claude Code's attempt to spawn `node .claude/statusline.js` fails visibly, once per response, surfaced by Claude Code itself as a command-spawn failure — this is a property of Claude Code's own command-invocation behavior, not code this feature controls, and it is a distinct condition from FR-13.6's "unconfigured" case (no status line at all, no failure signal); it is recorded here so the two are not assumed to degrade identically.

### 10.4 Non-Functional Requirements

1. **NFR-1 (autonomy contract, governing):** every capability MUST (a) fire from an existing pipeline point (`/develop-feature` Phase 0, an existing `/merge-ready` gate/preamble, `install.sh`, an existing agent's Process step) — FR-6's two new skills are the sole exception the roadmap names, bound to override-only use by FR-6.2/FR-6.3; (b) never dead-end an unattended run — FR-2 exists specifically so a tier boundary redirects rather than stops, except FR-2.5's ceiling, which restores today's already-compliant Rule 4 behavior; (c) fail open or degrade visibly on its own malfunction — FR-13.5 for the statusline.
2. **NFR-2 (no new mandatory pipeline stage):** FR-1's triage and FR-4.7's Tier Check preamble are additions inside `/develop-feature` and `/merge-ready`, not new skills a human must remember to invoke. FR-6's two skills are override entry points only — never invoked by the pipeline itself, never required for a run to complete.
3. **NFR-3 (backward compatibility):** a scratchpad with no `## Tier:` field (every scratchpad written before this feature — FR-2.8 makes this case decreasingly common going forward, since Step 7 now always writes the field) is treated as `full` by FR-4.7, FR-4.4, and every other tier-aware check — identical to today's unmodified behavior. A repository on which `install.sh --profile` has never been run behaves identically to today (FR-8.2, FR-9.3) — no observable change to `model:` values from installing this feature alone.
4. **NFR-4 (Node zones — extending Section 7 NFR-1's three-zone framework to a fourth case):** `install.sh`'s `--profile` logic (FR-7) stays inside the existing no-Node/no-jq zone — `sed`/`awk`/shell `case` only, verified by the existing CI step. `scripts/ci/validate-model-profile.js` (FR-10) is a normal member of the existing fail-closed `scripts/ci/` zone. `templates/statusline.js` (FR-13) is a NEW, fourth Node-execution context: invoked directly by Claude Code's own statusline renderer, never dispatched through `hooks/lib/run-hook.js`, not subject to Section 7's Node-version gate or `SDLC_HOOKS_ENABLED`/`SDLC_DISABLED_HOOKS` kill switches — it carries its own, separately-stated fail-open-by-analogy contract (FR-13.5). F4 adds no new hook.
5. **NFR-5 (asset budget):** agents remain at 14 (FR-11 adds a field to all 14 existing files, not a new agent). Skills move from 5 to 7 (FR-6.1), against ≤10. Hooks remain at 9 distinct ids / 10 `hooks.json` registrations (verified present at HEAD), against ≤12 — 3 ids of headroom remain, unchanged from Section 8's own accounting.
6. **NFR-6 (`effort:` is fixed, not profile-dependent):** the four model profiles (FR-8) govern only `model:`; `effort:` (FR-11) is assigned once, per role, at authoring time, and is never touched by `install.sh --profile`.

### 10.5 Acceptance Criteria

1. **AC-1:** A request reading "fix the typo 'recieve' to 'receive' in README.md" is classified `fast`, stating FR-1.4's single-file/copy-edit signal; a request reading "add a POST /api/webhooks/stripe endpoint" is classified `full`, stating FR-1.3(a)'s new-endpoint trigger — both reasons appear before any `Edit`/`Write` call (FR-1.8).
2. **AC-2:** A `fast`-tier single-line config-value change produces zero new/modified files under `docs/PRD.md`, `docs/use-cases/*`, or `docs/qa/*`; `CHANGELOG.md` gains exactly one new entry under today's date; `git log` shows exactly one new commit on a feature branch (FR-3.1, FR-3.3, FR-3.4).
3. **AC-3:** A `fast`-tier run whose second `Edit`/`Write` call targets a file outside the FR-1.2 estimated file set escalates to `quick` before that call is made — the response states the escalation and names the second file (FR-2.1, FR-2.3); `.claude/scratchpad.md` afterward shows `## Tier: quick`; the first edit remains, unreverted.
4. **AC-4:** A `quick`-tier run whose TDD cycle hits a Rule 4 condition does not stop and ask — it escalates to `full`: `.claude/scratchpad.md`'s `## Tier:` field reads `full` immediately upon escalation, before `/bootstrap-feature` is even invoked (FR-2.4(c)), and a context compaction immediately after escalation, followed by `session:start:spine`'s state re-injection, still reports `full` — never re-surfacing the pre-escalation `quick` value; `/bootstrap-feature` then runs for the expanded scope; the already-committed `quick`-tier slice appears in the resulting plan as DONE with its existing commit hash; and the run concludes through an unmodified, full 9-gate `/merge-ready` — verifiable as Gates 1, 5, 6, 7, and 8 actually running and reporting PASS/FAIL, never `SKIPPED (tier: quick)`, confirming the reduced gate subset was not silently applied to the escalated feature (FR-2.2, FR-2.4, FR-2.8).
5. **AC-5:** A `quick`-tier run that in fact touched only 1 file still reports Gates 1/5/6/7/8 as `SKIPPED (tier: quick)`, never silently re-labeled `fast` after the fact — verifiable by inspecting the scratchpad's `## Tier:` value against the value set at the run's start or its one permitted escalation (FR-2.6).
6. **AC-6:** A request containing the prose "let's do this quickly" but invoking neither `/sdlc-fast` nor `/sdlc-quick` is classified by FR-1's normal signals — the stated reason cites an FR-1.3/1.4/1.5/1.6 signal, never "override requested." A literal `/sdlc-quick <description>` invocation, by contrast, skips FR-1 and states so (FR-6.2, FR-6.3).
7. **AC-7:** `install.sh --profile budget` without `--local` exits non-zero, naming the `--local` requirement, and modifies no file (FR-7.1). After `install.sh --local --profile budget`, all 14 `agents/*.md` files' `model:` lines equal FR-8.1's `budget` column values, with every other line of every file byte-identical to before; because 6 of the 14 roles' `budget` value equals their `quality` baseline (`security-auditor`, `ba-analyst`, `code-reviewer`, `e2e-runner`, `qa-planner`, `verifier`), `git diff --stat` afterward shows exactly the other **8** files as changed — `architect.md`, `plan-critic.md`, `planner.md`, `build-runner.md`, `doc-updater.md`, `prd-writer.md`, `refactor-cleaner.md`, `test-writer.md` — one changed line each, and `.sdlc-model-profile` reads exactly `budget` (FR-7.2, FR-7.4, FR-9.1).
8. **AC-8:** `install.sh --local --profile budget --dry-run` prints all 14 current/target value pairs and exits 0 with no file modified — `git status` afterward is clean (FR-7.5).
9. **AC-9:** After `install.sh --local --profile budget`, hand-editing `agents/architect.md`'s `model:` to `fable` makes `node scripts/ci/validate-model-profile.js` exit non-zero, naming the file, the found value `fable`, and the expected value `sonnet` — while `node scripts/ci/validate-agents.js` continues to exit 0 against the same file, since `fable` remains a recognized alias under that check's separate, looser purpose (FR-10.2).
10. **AC-10:** `node scripts/ci/validate-model-profile.js` exits 0 against the real repository tree with no `.sdlc-model-profile` present (FR-8.2, FR-9.3), and exits non-zero against each of FR-10.4's three seeded-bad fixtures, each failing for the specific reason its fixture encodes (`--expect-failure` substring match).
11. **AC-11:** The existing "Assert install.sh invokes neither node nor jq" CI step continues to exit 0 after FR-7 lands — `grep -nE '(^|[^a-zA-Z])(node|jq)([^a-zA-Z]|$)' install.sh | grep -v '^[0-9]*: *#'` on the modified file produces empty output (FR-7.2's sed/awk-only idiom).
12. **AC-12:** `grep -l "effort: low" agents/*.md` returns exactly 3 files; `grep -l "effort: high" agents/*.md` returns exactly 5 files; `grep -l "effort: medium" agents/*.md` returns exactly the remaining 6 — matching FR-11.2 exactly (mirroring Section 3's original AC-1/AC-2 grep precedent).
13. **AC-13:** After `install.sh --local --profile inherit`, every agent file reads `model: inherit` literally (not an absent field), and `node scripts/ci/validate-agents.js`'s existing `REQUIRED_FIELDS` check passes rather than failing on a missing field (FR-7.3).
14. **AC-14:** A `planner` invocation for a new, third `full`-tier feature — with `docs/digest-index.md` already holding 5+ rows — reads only the current feature's own PRD section (not the whole `docs/PRD.md`) plus between 2 and 4 prior rows in full, never all 5+ (FR-12.3, FR-12.4).
15. **AC-15:** A `planner` invocation for the first `full`-tier feature in a fresh project (`docs/digest-index.md` absent) proceeds with 0 prior-feature reads and does not stall for lack of 2 relevant entries (FR-12.4's fallback).
16. **AC-16:** A `full`-tier feature's Gate 7 run appends exactly one new row to `docs/digest-index.md`; re-running Gate 7 for the same feature updates that row in place rather than duplicating it (FR-12.2). A `quick`-tier feature's completion (Gate 7 `SKIPPED`) produces no digest row (FR-12.6).
17. **AC-17:** In a live session with `statusLine` configured, an active `full`-tier run at wave 2 of 3 / slice 1 of 2, with `/merge-ready` having completed 4 of 9 gates, renders all five FR-13.3 segments, with `wave 2 slice 1/2` and `gates 4/9` matching the scratchpad's actual state.
18. **AC-18:** With no active feature and no in-progress `/merge-ready` run, the status line omits the wave/slice and gates segments entirely (never `0/0`), and still renders cost and usable-context (FR-13.3).

21. **AC-21:** `ls agents/*.md | wc -l` remains 14, `ls skills/*/SKILL.md | wc -l` reads 7, `ls hooks/handlers/*.js | wc -l` remains 9 after this feature ships (NFR-5).

23. **AC-23:** A `quick`-tier run implementing "add a missing validation" whose fix, once underway, requires editing auth middleware — a path FR-1.7 marks sensitive — escalates to `full` before the `Edit` call touching that path is made, per FR-2.2(b), with the same postconditions AC-4 verifies (tier rewritten to `full` before `/bootstrap-feature` runs, full 9-gate `/merge-ready` at completion).

26. **AC-26:** A `quick`-tier run's `/implement-slice` Step 2 delegation to `test-writer` states, verbatim, that no `docs/qa/<feature>_test_cases.md` file exists by design; `test-writer` writes tests derived from the slice's own `Verify:`/`Done when:` fields rather than reporting a missing input (FR-4.4).
27. **AC-27:** A `quick`-tier plan's single slice, which carries no `**Tracer:** yes` marker (FR-4.1), causes `/implement-slice` to print the tier-aware notice naming `tier: quick`, never the "treating as pre-F3 plan" legacy notice (FR-4.5).
28. **AC-28:** `skills/sdlc-fast/SKILL.md`'s frontmatter `allowed-tools` includes `Agent`; an `/sdlc-fast` run whose edit touches a second file successfully escalates to `quick` — invoking `planner` via the `Agent` tool — rather than being blocked by its own tool grant (FR-6.1).
29. **AC-29:** A fixture run of `install.sh --local --profile budget` against a copy of `agents/*.md` where one file (e.g., `agents/architect.md`) has had its `model:` line deleted entirely fails preflight before any of the 14 files is modified — verifiable as `git status` showing zero changed files and a non-zero exit code naming the file missing its `model:` line (FR-7.2(c)/(d)).
30. **AC-30:** The FR-7.6 spike's finding — whether a running Claude Code session re-reads `agents/*.md` frontmatter live or snapshots it at plugin load — is recorded in `install.sh`'s own header comment and in a README note stating plainly whether a new session or a `/plugin` reinstall is required after `install.sh --profile` for the new `model:` values to take effect.
31. **AC-31:** A project's `.claude/rules/security.md` declaring `## Sensitive Paths` with only `docs/marketing/**` listed still classifies a request touching `src/auth/login.ts` as sensitive under the fixed default (FR-1.7) — the declared entry adds to, and never replaces, the default list.
32. **AC-32:** `node scripts/ci/validate-triage-parity.js` exits 0 against the real repository tree (the Steps 1-7 blocks in `skills/develop-feature/SKILL.md` and `src/claude.md` are byte-identical at HEAD); exits non-zero against a seeded fixture where one copy's Step 4 has been hand-edited to diverge from the other, naming Step 4 specifically as the differing step (not merely "files differ"); and exits non-zero against an empty/absent tree (`v.requireMinimum` anti-vacuity, mirroring every other validator in `scripts/ci/`) (FR-1.9).
